# everyday – Habit Tracker (App Store ID 1394150432) — Full Review Analysis

**App:** everyday – Habit Tracker — *Daily Routine Checklist*
**Developer of record:** Everyday Growth S.L. · **Bundle:** `app.everyday`
**Corpus:** 2,178 written reviews · 93 storefronts · 9 August 2018 → 4 September 2026
**Corpus mean rating:** 4.332 · **Extracted:** 8 September 2026 (`itunes MZStore userReviewsRow`)
**Analysis date:** 11 September 2026
**Business model:** Free download with a **3-habit free tier**, a **7-day trial**, and paid **auto-renewing subscriptions plus a lifetime unlock**. Store listing (US, accessed 11 September 2026) shows *ED Premium Monthly Plan* **$7.49**, *Everyday Premium Year* **$29.99**, *ED Full Year Access* **$29.99** and *Everyday Premium Lifetime* **$99.99**. Reviewers document a price ladder: **$12/year (2019) → $4.99/month or $30/year (January 2020) → $7.49/month, $29.99/year, $99.99 lifetime (2023 onward)**. The free-tier cap of 3 habits is described in the first cap complaint (January 2019) and in the listing today — **it never changed across the corpus**.

---

## How to read this

- Every quantified finding gives **count, percentage, denominator, scope, period, signal label, and review IDs**. Every claim traces to an exact record via `review_id` in `reviews.jsonl`. **§9.7 is a complete 2,178-row index** — every review in the corpus with its ID, storefront, star rating, date, verbatim title and assigned themes — and **§9.8 lists the full review-ID set for every one of the 109 themes.** Nothing in this report is unreachable from those two tables.
- Theme counts are **non-exclusive** — one review can carry several themes. All 2,178 records carry at least one theme (205 carry `meta_low_information`, most of them as their only theme).
- Signal thresholds: `<0.1%` ignore · `0.1–0.5%` weak · `0.5–1%` emerging · `1–3%` meaningful · `3–5%` very strong · `>5%` high-priority. Denominator for every global percentage is **2,178**; for each of the ten eligible storefronts it is that storefront's own count (§6).
- **Facts** are what reviewers wrote. **Interpretation** is labelled as such.
- The developer is referred to by reviewers as "Joan" and is identified by several of them as an independent developer in Barcelona. This report uses the name only where a reviewer does, and uses "they" throughout.

### ⚠️ Eight warnings before any number here is used

1. **This corpus is overwhelmingly positive.** 1,532 of 2,178 reviews are 5★ (**70.34%**) and 253 are 4★ (11.62%) — **81.96% of written reviews are 4★ or 5★**. Only 250 (11.48%) are 1★ or 2★. Findings about what is wrong with the product rest on much smaller absolute counts than findings about what is right, and are flagged accordingly.
2. **The corpus mean (4.332) is below the store's displayed aggregate (4.7 from 4.5K ratings on the US storefront, accessed 11 September 2026).** The US storefront contributes 728 written reviews against ~4.5K US ratings — written reviews are roughly one rating in six there. **Do not report 4.332 as this app's rating.**
3. **A solicited-review burst is present and is disclosed rather than dropped.** **297 reviews (13.64% of the corpus) were posted in 44 days, 18 May → 30 June 2023** — 24 on 19 May and 24 on 21 May alone. The burst coincides exactly with the arrival of an aggressive in-app rating prompt: the window is **5.4× richer in prompt complaints** (23 of 297, 7.7%, vs 27 of 1,881, 1.4%) and **2.5× richer in contentless reviews** (`meta_low_information` 19.5% vs 7.8%). Its mean is **4.58**, against **4.29** for the rest of the corpus. Every record is retained in every denominator; where the burst distorts a figure, the figure is given with and without it. Removing the burst moves the global mean from 4.332 to **4.293 (n=1,881)**; removing the burst and all contentless reviews gives **4.251 (n=1,734)**. **Interpretation: in-app rating prompts are permitted by Apple and this is not evidence of fake reviews — but it means post-May-2023 theme *rates* are diluted by a wave of short, prompted 5★ reviews, and Part 7 corrects for it.**
4. **Ten storefronts clear the 50-review bar**: United States (728), China (136), Spain (127), Canada (99), Germany (84), United Kingdom (84), Russia (74), Brazil (67), India (58), Turkey (54). All ten are analysed individually (§6). France (48), Mexico (48), Australia (47) and Italy (46) fall just short and are presented as **limited evidence** only.
5. **This is the least US-concentrated corpus in the series so far.** The US is **33.43%** of records (report 43: 64.11%). 1,132 reviews (51.97%) come from non-English-primary storefronts. Reviews were read in their original language — English, Simplified and Traditional Chinese, Spanish, **Catalan** (at least 11 reviews), German, Russian, Ukrainian, Portuguese, Turkish, French, Italian, Dutch, Korean, Japanese, Polish, Czech, Arabic, Persian, Swedish, Danish, Thai, Vietnamese, Romanian, Croatian, Slovenian and Indonesian. Translations are given beside every non-English quotation.
6. **Classification was hand-curated, not blind-automated.** All 2,178 records were read individually, in full, in date order. Every theme is a hand-assigned ID list, not regex output. No sampling. **109 themes across 11 families, 4,234 assignments (mean 1.94 per review).** Eight themes were introduced partway through the read; five of them were then applied back to earlier records they clearly fit, and a second-look pass while drafting corrected eight records where "replaces paper" had been assigned to reviews saying the opposite — **22 records patched in all**. Every patch is listed, with its reason, at the foot of the classification file. Boundary rules are in §9.1.
7. **31 records (1.42%) have a star rating that contradicts their own text** — `meta_rating_text_mismatch`. Seven of them (5.15% of the storefront) are Chinese 5★ reviews reporting that the app cannot connect (e.g. `4836234866`, 5★, *"显示离线模式，不让记录"* / "Shows offline mode, won't let me record"); others are 1★ reviews that praise the app (`11259213795`, 1★, *"Love this app! This app is keeping me true to my goals"*) or that punish the review prompt (`11090018321`, FR, 1★, *"J'aime everyday. Je n'aime pas qu'on me force la main pour laisser un avis"* / "I like everyday. I don't like being forced to leave a review"). All are retained; rating-only analysis of this corpus carries roughly 1.4% noise.
8. **No conversion rate can be inferred from review text and none is claimed.** 97 reviewers (4.45%) give first-person evidence of having paid, subscribed, bought the lifetime tier, or been charged. That is a self-selected sample of writers, not of buyers.

---

# EXECUTIVE SUMMARY

**1. The 3-habit free tier is the defining negative in this corpus, and — unlike report 43's app — this developer never loosened it.**
**180 reviews (8.26%, high-priority, mean 2.43) complain about the 3-habit cap.** It is the largest negative theme, it appears in **68 of the 166 one-star reviews (41.0%)** and **29 of the 84 two-stars (34.5%)**, and it runs unbroken from the first complaint (`3621518582`, US, 3★, Jan 2019, *"I found that I could set up only 3 entries"*) to the last (`13938765242`, TR, 3★, Apr 2026, *"I'm disappointed why only 3 habits? Just to make more money ?"*). The listing still says *"Free version limited to 3 habits."* Its annual **count** barely moved — 26 (2022), 32 (2023), 22 (2024), 18 (2025) — and its **rate** fell from 16.2% of reviews (Jan 2021–Apr 2023) to 5.2% (2024–2026) mostly because total review volume tripled after the rating prompt arrived (Warning 3). Adding the reviewers who say the free tier is too small to evaluate (`mf_trial_too_limited`, 32), that the listing's "free" is misleading (`mf_misleading_free_claim`, 27, mean 1.85), and who ask for 4–6 free habits (`mf_more_free_habits_request`, 29), **the paywall union is 204 reviews (9.37%, mean 2.47)**. Representative: *"By the time you discover that you can only track 3 habits with the free version, you've already spent 20 minutes or more setting it up"* (`8247232385`, US, 1★, 16 helpful votes); *"3 hábitos y pasar por caja. Borrada"* / "3 habits and then pay up. Deleted" (`6035060089`, ES, 1★); *"I just beg of the developer please at the very least increase it from 3 habits limit to 4… when I use the widget (which fits 4 habits) it leaves the 4th row blank"* (`9997347192`, US, 4★). **Against this, 66 reviewers (3.03%, mean 4.92) say three is enough** — *"For those complaining for tracking only three habits while it's free, shouldn't be tracking more than three in the first place"* (`5655839185`, US, 5★). **Interpretation: the ask is modest — four to six habits, not unlimited — and report 43 is the natural experiment showing that loosening a 3-item cap removes the theme within about two years. The widget point is a concrete design defect: the free tier cannot fill the product's own 4-row widget.**

**2. Price is judged against simplicity, and the same simplicity 425 people praise is the argument 163 people use against paying.**
**163 reviews (7.48%, high-priority, mean 2.84) say the price or subscription is too high**; the pricing-model union (price, one-time ask, anti-subscription, lifetime price, price increase, cheaper competitor, regional price, discount request) is **212 reviews (9.73%, mean 2.92)**. The core argument is value-per-feature: *"$4.99 a month is too much to ask for the ability to electronically check a box"* (`5111990858`, US, 1★); *"for an app that is essentially a checkbox $30 (a year) is way too much"* (`7060353342`, US, 2★); *"350 for a year for a considerably basic app is too much. can just go do it in a spreadsheet"* (`6379337258`, SE, 2★). Reviewers benchmark against specific products — Spotify (`6162571234`, BR, *"R$17,90 is twice the Spotify subscription"*), Adobe Lightroom (`6248048625`), 200 GB of iCloud and Microsoft Office (`6859946614`), 1Password (`8226941128`), Notion and (Not Boring) Habits (`9882563220`) — and name cheaper habit trackers they moved to (`mf_competitor_cheaper`, 28, mean 2.25). A price rise is recorded in the corpus itself: *"Price went from $1/month to $5/month, or from $12/year to $30/year. Most of the 'worth it' reviews reflect the original pricing"* (`5471831095`, US, 1★, Jan 2020, 12 helpful votes). **Interpretation: this is a positioning problem as much as a price problem. A product marketed on "simple" invites a "simple things should be cheap" comparison; the feature set the premium tier adds is not what reviewers talk about.**

**3. The one-time-purchase request was answered with a lifetime tier — and the lifetime price is now the objection.**
`mf_want_one_time_purchase` (53, 2.43%, mean 3.32) runs **6.99% of reviews in 2018–2020 → 4.74% → 0.38% → 0.68% (2024–2026)**; across halves **4.22% → 0.64%**. The first lifetime purchase appears in May 2021 (`7299054449`, US, 5★, *"I paid for the lifetime membership and I couldnt be happier"*). As the one-time ask disappears, `mf_lifetime_price_too_high` appears in its place — **27 reviews (1.24%, meaningful, mean 3.00), 0 before July 2022, 0.46% → 2.02% across halves.** Reviewers name the price at which they would buy, and they converge: *"I have paid for life-time subscription for a few [habit trackers] but I have never seen someone charge more than $30 for it… I will be happy to pay $30 for life-time"* (`10650959929`, US, 3★); *"Would probably buy it lifetime if it was 30 or less"* (`11735469517`, US, 4★); *"make it a one time $30 lifetime cost which I'm sure many people will gladly pay. However, asking for almost $100 for lifetime is GREEDY"* (`10453985829`, US, 3★, a multi-year subscriber); *"69.99 and I probably would have paid"* (`12101755389`, US, 3★, who took the annual plan instead); *"在一众30元终身的app里要328元，实在是不敢买怕跑路"* / "Among apps that charge ¥30 for lifetime, this one wants ¥328 — I really don't dare buy it, in case it disappears" (`11206877099`, CN, 3★). The lifetime tier does convert: **16 reviewers (0.73%) say they bought it, mean 4.56** — *"几年前就下载过，当时因为没有lifetime的付款方式，就没成为付费用户。这回有lifetime的付费方式了，果断买了"* / "I downloaded it years ago but didn't pay because there was no lifetime option. Now there is, so I bought it straight away" (`12237402875`, CN, 5★). **Interpretation: the corpus contains an unusually specific willingness-to-pay anchor — roughly $30–70 for lifetime, stated independently by at least four reviewers against a $99.99 list price. This is the single best-evidenced pricing experiment in the report (Part 8, E1).**

**4. The colour-saturation grid is the product, and it is the reason people choose it over named competitors.**
**228 reviews (10.47%, high-priority, mean 4.86) say the colour system itself is what motivates them** — squares that deepen with each consecutive completion. It is joined by `cp_streak_motivation` (44), `cp_skip_forgiving` (41, mean 4.93 — the half-square and triangle "skip" marks) and `cp_competitive_displacement` (198, 9.09%, mean 4.92 — "I tried many, this one won"). *"Le système des couleurs qui évoluent en fonction de la réussite des habitudes est hyper motivant"* / "The colour system that evolves with your success is hugely motivating" (`3499180279`, FR, 5★, 2018); *"因为不想让渐变色断开，所以每天会有动力去完成打卡"* / "Because I don't want the gradient to break, I'm motivated to check in every day" (`5595638800`, CN, 5★); *"Instead of filling in the rectangle, it puts a triangle in that space, and that feels like neutral data instead of a personal failing"* (`10483508663`, US, 5★, ADHD, a year of use); *"Anybody that like seeing a git commit graph will love this app"* (`9567101427`, US, 5★). **Interpretation: the moat is a visual mechanic, not a feature list — which is why reviewers can call the same product "revolutionary" (`7033733479`) and "a bunch of coloured blocks" (`6998451648`). Every recommendation in Part 8 protects this surface.**

**5. The most-requested feature is more colours, and it is the cheapest request in the corpus to satisfy.**
**`fr_more_colours` — 59 reviews (2.71%, meaningful, mean 4.36), 35 of them 5★ and 15 more 4★.** It is the top feature request in every era after 2020. The asks are specific: a hex-code picker (*"I would love very much to have the option to enter a hex code… I will pay for this feature!!"* — `9952382181`, US, 4★, tracking 21 habits; `11699632553`, RU), pastel and neutral palettes (`9944097015`, `10745919163`, `8194661184`), *"more (than 7) default colors"* (`8634977816`, PH; `11366056883`, US), colours for 21 habits (`11794827684`, US), and a paid palette: *"I would totally splurge a buck 99 for a rain themed color pallete"* (`12101755389`, US). Four more reviews say long streaks turn colours muddy (`ux_colour_gets_muddy`, e.g. `13866974245`, DE, *"Yellow disappears completely and turns red. Red turns brown. Green turns olive"*). **Interpretation: this is a fifth-star request from people who already love the product, and at least two volunteer to pay for it. A palette pack is also the only in-app purchase the corpus asks for unprompted.**

**6. Data loss is the most severe defect, it is rising, and its most recent cluster is concentrated in one storefront.**
**24 reviews (1.10%, meaningful, mean 2.12) report checked squares, streaks, habits or history disappearing or being rewritten.** The rate doubles across halves (**0.73% → 1.47%**) and 13 of the 24 fall in 2024–2026. **8 of the 24 are paying customers.** All four data-loss reviews dated 2026 are from the **Russian storefront, 7 February → 1 March 2026**: `13721978399` (3★, *"пропадают отметки, проставленные пару дней, а то и неделю назад"* / "marks from a couple of days or even a week ago disappear"), `13741293681` (2★, subscriber, *"может сбросить последние 3-4 дня. С подпиской"* / "it can wipe the last 3–4 days. With a subscription"), `13765557194` (2★, subscriber, *"деньги тоже никто не собирался возвращать… минус 1000₽"* / "nobody was going to refund me… minus ₽1000"), `13802922048` (3★). Elsewhere: *"I used this app for two months and it suddenly reset and lost all my data. What am I paying $29.99/year for…? I want a refund"* (`11748480154`, US, 1★); *"every 1 or 2 months it suddenly loses almost all progress"* (`13339379159`, AU, 3★); *"the results for habit one are somehow already displayed for habit three… it's essentially nonfunctional"* (`13416396839`, ID, 1★, paid); *"The widgets constantly display wrong info and the app itself just randomly changes input. I REALLY regret buying this"* (`12356330098`, BE, 2★, €99 lifetime). The broader **data-integrity union** (data loss, sync failures, widgets showing stale or wrong data, accidental taps) is **56 reviews (2.57%)**. **Interpretation: for an app whose entire value is an unbroken visual record, silently rewritten history is the highest-severity failure possible. The February 2026 Russian cluster should be treated as a live incident until release logs say otherwise.**

**7. The in-app rating prompt is the largest self-inflicted problem, and it produced about one review in seven in this corpus.**
**`ux_review_prompt_nag` — 50 reviews (2.30%, meaningful, mean 3.22), including 10 one-stars.** It first appears in January 2022 (`8258958104`, GB, *"keeps nagging me to rate it… even though I've rated it about five times already"*), explodes with the May 2023 burst (30 of 50 fall in May–December 2023), and has **not stopped**: the latest is `14423865619` (US, 5★, 13 August 2026, *"i already submitted 5 stars stop asking me pls"*). *"App asks me to review 4 times in 10 minutes. I haven't even setup 2 habits yet. Only option is 'Not now'… Think twice before adding dark patterns into your app"* (`12129865860`, US, 2★); *"Every 3 actions it asks me to rate the app. I've been using it for 5 minutes! Chill"* (`11474568668`, GB, 1★); *"continui a chiedermi la recensione? -> ti punisco"* / "you keep asking me for a review? → I'm punishing you" (`9975055378`, IT, 1★, body: *"miao"*). One reviewer was told in June 2023 that it had been fixed (`10008701823`); 19 more complaints follow in 2024–2026. A further **17 reviews (`meta_solicited`, 0.78%)** say outright that they are writing only to make the prompt stop. **Interpretation: the prompt is a rating-acquisition mechanism that is generating one-star reviews, and it has made the post-2023 corpus less informative per review. The fix is a single "don't ask again" state.**

**8. Paying customers rate the app lower than non-payers, and the gap is caused by delivery and billing failures, not by the product.**
**97 reviewers (4.45%, very strong) give first-person evidence of paying** — 74 subscribers, 16 lifetime buyers, 13 who report being charged. Their mean is **3.95** against **4.35** for the 2,081 others; **12.4% of payers leave 1★** against 7.4% of non-payers. **21 of 97 payers (21.6% segment rate; 0.96% of the corpus) hit a reliability defect after paying**, including 8 data-loss and 6 sync failures. **13 reviews (0.60%, emerging, mean 2.31) report a billing failure**: charged on day one of a "free trial" (`11022906761`, US, 1★, *"Offerred 7 day trial, but I got charged the same day"*; `12285802750`, US, 1★), a subscription the app would not recognise (`6866992048`, US, 1★; `14423487007`, US, 1★, Aug 2026, *"$29 and it won't even recognize the paid subscription. I reached out to 'Joan' via email and didn't get a response"*; `6863161594`, TW, 1★, premium on iPad but not on iPhone), a wrong amount (`10373652027`, CH, 3★, *"I was charged CHF 40.45 instead of CHF 29.95"*) and three refund requests (`6593564259`, `11748480154`, `13765557194`). **Interpretation: report 43's one-time-purchase app recorded 2 refund references in 638 reviews. A subscription with a trial has more ways to fail at the exact moment money moves, and this corpus shows each of them.**

**9. Support is personal and widely praised — but the developer's public replies to price criticism are converting critics into one-star reviewers.**
**53 reviews (2.43%, meaningful, mean 4.94) praise support**, usually naming the developer: *"I was able to reach out to Joan and received a response in less than 30 minutes"* (`7232471445`, US, 5★); *"Joan helped me add my previous habit data from a different app"* (`12948882804`, US, 5★, raised from 4★); eight reviews record a rating raised after contact (`5385110766`, `5972193537`, `9360709496`, `9667767203`, `10074816469`, `12349381251`, `12948882804`, `14054333465`). Against this, **6 reviews (0.28%, weak, mean 1.33) say a public developer reply was rude or defensive** — all 2021 or later, five of them 2024–2026 — and **2 say the developer pressed them to change their review**: *"snarky remarks about 'working for free' and making an overpriced app isn't the way to do it"* (`11034917382`, US, 1★); *"developer takes any criticism with such a nasty attitude"* (`11113145328`, US, 1★); *"After reading developer's response I am degrading rating to 1 star from 2… 5/5 rating for app but 0/5 for your behaviour"* (`13298363427`, IN, 1★); *"the developer keeps contacting me to pressure me into changing my review"* (`11709301622`, US, 1★). A further 7 (0.32%, mean 1.57) say support never answered. **Interpretation: small in count, but every one of these is a reviewer who went from "too expensive" to "and the developer is hostile" — a reputational cost no pricing change recovers. Reply policy on price reviews is a zero-cost fix.**

**10. China is a distinct market with its own defect, its own purchase preference, and its own fear.**
**China (n=136, 6.24%) is the second-largest storefront.** Three things are specific to it: **(a)** `rel_offline_mode_bug` — **16 of the corpus's 17 "offline mode while online" reports are Chinese (11.76% of CN)**, nine of them in the nine days 20–28 September 2019 (`4808959996` → `4848868262`) and five more in March–April 2020; **(b)** a one-time-purchase preference — **15 of the 53 `mf_want_one_time_purchase` reviews (28.3%) are Chinese (11.03% of CN)**, e.g. *"如果有一次性完整付费，我会更愿意掏钱"* / "If there were a one-time full purchase, I'd be more willing to pay" (`5912213663`); **(c)** `mf_longevity_doubt` — **all 5 reviews in the corpus that hesitate to pay for fear the app will vanish are Chinese**, using the word 跑路 ("run off"): *"之前用过一个类似的软件还支付了费用，结果跑路了，希望你们别跑"* / "I paid for a similar app before and it ran off — I hope you don't" (`5210358729`). **Interpretation, labelled: the 2019–2020 offline cluster is consistent with the app's sync backend being intermittently unreachable from mainland China. It has not recurred at volume since 2020 (two instances in 2023). The purchase-shape preference is real and is a separate question from price.**

**11. Scheduling flexibility is the longest-running functional request.**
**`fr_flexible_frequency` — 34 reviews (1.56%, meaningful, mean 4.09)** — every-other-day, every-3-days, X-times-per-week without fixed days, monthly — plus **12 asking for a habit completable several times a day** and **9 for numeric targets** (glasses, steps, pages). It is present in every era (1.08% → 2.00% → 1.71% → 1.48%). *"I have a medicine I must use every other day and I can't put it on this app, which I use for virtually every other reminder"* (`10205085824`, US, 5★, 8 helpful votes); *"I'm a shift worker and don't always know which days I'll be able to perform a specific task… this could potentially be a deal breaker"* (`10429962976`, US, 4★, on trial); *"most of my habit are repeated every 3 days… Apple reminders is WAY more efficient"* (`8804092407`, FR, 2★, churned). **Interpretation: these reviewers are 4★–5★ users hitting a model limit, and one names the free alternative they left for.**

**12. ADHD and neurodivergent users are a small, warm, mostly US segment — and the mechanism they name is the forgiving skip.**
**30 reviews (1.38%, meaningful, mean 4.83) self-identify with ADHD, OCD, autism, dyslexia, bipolar disorder or chronic illness; 23 are US.** The segment grows from 0.54% of 2018–2020 reviews to 1.70% of 2024–2026 reviews. The features it names are the skip mark and the widget: *"It could be the ability to still 'log' the days you don't do something… that feels like neutral data instead of a personal failing"* (`10483508663`); *"With my brain, if I don't see something I forget it exists… The widgets are my favorite part"* (`9961143974`, US, 5★); *"I've got chronic pain and having something forgiving is very nice. Completely functional without paying! No ads!"* (`12222718804`, US, 5★); *"as an individual affected by dyslexia… the progress… [is] simply shown by diluted or intensified colors"* (`11918274646`, US, 5★). **Interpretation: the forgiving-skip design is doing real accessibility work. Any change to how misses are displayed (see §3.5, `fr_missed_vs_blank`) must be tested against this segment first.**

**13. The early-adopter community is being diluted, and the things it valued are fading from the corpus.**
Four themes tied to the 2018–2020 user base all fall: **Learn-tab content and the 7-day email course** (`cp_learn_content`, 107, 4.91%, mean 4.82) **11.02% → 5.24% → 3.05% → 3.30%**; **web-app / Chrome-extension users** (`seg_web_or_extension_user`) **2.15% → 0.23%**; **praise for responsive support** **4.30% → 1.82%**; **supporting an indie developer as a reason to pay** (`mp_support_indie`) **12 of 13 before July 2023**. **Interpretation, medium confidence: the early user was a desktop-heavy, newsletter-reading, developer-contacting habit enthusiast; the post-2023 user is a mobile, widget-first, prompt-driven reviewer. The content library the early users praised is under-used rather than disliked — and 13 reviews (0.60%) say it is only in English, including a Spanish reviewer surprised because *"el desarrollador es español"* (`8985834449`).**

**14. The cheapest high-value moves, in evidence order:** add a permanent "don't ask again" to the rating prompt (50 complaints, 10 one-stars, still live in August 2026) → treat the February 2026 Russian data-loss cluster as an incident and ship a visible history-integrity check (24 data-loss reviews, 8 from payers) → fix trial billing and subscription recognition (13 billing reports, including two "charged on day one of the trial") → stop arguing price in public replies (6 hostile-reply reviews, all converted to 1–2★) → raise the free tier to 4 habits so it fills the 4-row widget (29 explicit requests for 4–6; the widget defect in `9997347192`, `12334331759`) → ship more colours or a paid palette pack (59 requests, mean 4.36) → test a lifetime price near the $30–70 band reviewers name (27 lifetime-price objections) → add every-N-days and X-per-week scheduling (34 requests).

---

# PART 1 — SCOPE AND METHOD

## 1.1 Files used

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `reviews.jsonl` | Primary corpus — 2,178 records, one JSON object per line |
| `by_country/*.jsonl` | 93 per-storefront files — reconciled exactly to the main file |
| `manifest.json` | App metadata, per-storefront counts, rating distribution, mean |
| `_state.json` | Extraction state; all 143 listed storefronts are `complete: true`; 50 of them returned zero written reviews and 93 returned at least one. Used only to confirm that zero-review storefronts were actually queried. |
| App Store listing (external) | `https://apps.apple.com/us/app/everyday-habit-tracker/id1394150432`, accessed 11 September 2026 — used only for Part 2 price/feature/language/platform confirmation, always labelled |
| App-market consumer-spend context (external) | Publicly reported App Store consumer-spend market rankings, accessed 11 September 2026 — used only to define the high-spend group in §6.12, always labelled; the same definition as report 43 |

## 1.2 Schema

Fields present on every one of the 2,178 records: `review_id`, `app_id`, `app_name`, `country`, `country_name`, `rating`, `title`, `body`, `author`, `date`, `vote_count`, `vote_sum`, `is_edited`.

**Fields used in this analysis:** `review_id`, `country`, `rating`, `title`, `body`, `date`.
**Fields inspected and set aside:** `author` (not analysed; no reviewer is named in this report), `app_id` / `app_name` / `country_name` (constant or derivable), `vote_sum` (differs from `vote_count` on 79 records by small numbers of down-votes; no finding uses it).
**Fields inspected and used only as an integrity check:**
- `vote_count` — 1,939 of 2,178 records (89.03%) have zero helpful votes. The most-voted reviews are `6181587486` (IT, 4★, 25 votes — a notification that keeps firing after the habit is deleted), `6164086392` (TR, 4★, 16 — a request for Turkish), `8247232385` (US, 1★, 16 — the 3-habit cap discovered after 20 minutes of setup), `6735020315` (US, 5★, 15 — a month of progress, Duolingo comparison), `6198632744` (AU, 5★, 13) and `5471831095` (US, 1★, 12 — the January 2020 price rise). **Votes did not surface the May 2023 burst**; it was found by date clustering (Warning 3).
- `is_edited` — true on 67 records (3.08%). Where an edit changed the verdict it is noted in-line (e.g. `5385110766` raised to 5★ after developer contact; `9667767203` changed from 1★ to 5★ after a widget fix; `6998451648` edited twice, each time angrier, after a developer reply).

**Text handling:** HTML entities (`&#39;`, `&#34;`, `&amp;`) in `title` and `body` were unescaped before reading. Body length: median **93.5 characters**, mean 159, minimum 1 (`10014155915`, body "🤩"), maximum 1,749 (`9772705531`, US, 2★).

## 1.3 Coverage and reconciliation

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Lines in `reviews.jsonl` | **2,178** |
| Records parsed successfully | **2,178 (100%)** |
| Unique `review_id` | **2,178 — zero duplicate IDs** |
| Records with empty `body` or `title` | 0 |
| Records missing any schema field | 0 |
| Records summed across `by_country/*.jsonl` | **2,178**, all unique, zero cross-file duplicates |
| IDs in country files not present in main file | **0** |
| IDs in main file not present in any country file | **0** |
| Records where `country` field ≠ country filename | **0** |
| Exact duplicate (title, body) pairs | 3 groups, 12 records — "Good" / "Good" ×7; "good app" / "good app" ×3; "hao" / "hao" ×2 — different reviewers, retained |
| **Records read individually and classified** | **2,178 (100%)** |
| Records carrying ≥1 assigned theme | **2,178 (100%)** |
| Themes assigned | 109, across 10 families |
| Unknown review IDs in the classification map | 0 |
| Duplicate keys in the classification file | 0 |
| Themes assigned twice to the same review | 0 |
| Themes defined but never used | 0 |
| Denominator used for every global percentage | **2,178** |

`manifest.json` states 2,178 total reviews, 93 storefronts with reviews, mean 4.332 and a rating distribution of 5:1532 / 4:253 / 3:143 / 2:84 / 1:166. **Independent recomputation from the raw records reproduces all of these exactly**, and every one of the 93 per-country counts reproduces exactly against the merged file.

**Deduplication: none performed.** The duplicate (title, body) pairs above are different reviewers writing the same one or two words; they are separate reviews with separate IDs, not technical duplicates. Every percentage uses 2,178 as its denominator.

## 1.4 Date range and shape

- First review: **9 August 2018** (`3038410981`, US, 1★ — *"Unable to log in or sign up. I fill out the form, and the submit button is inactive"*)
- Last review: **4 September 2026** (`14509933384`, MN, 3★ — *"It's good"*)
- Span: **8 years 1 month.** Reviewers in 2018 describe a web app that preceded the iPhone release (`3197455014`, `3200513030`).

| Year | n | % of corpus | Mean ★ | 5★ | 4★ | 3★ | 2★ | 1★ |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2018 | 12 | 0.6% | 4.67 | 11 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 1 |
| 2019 | 86 | 3.9% | 3.87 | 48 | 10 | 11 | 3 | 14 |
| 2020 | 274 | 12.6% | 4.08 | 160 | 43 | 28 | 18 | 25 |
| 2021 | 177 | 8.1% | 4.09 | 113 | 17 | 16 | 12 | 19 |
| 2022 | 140 | 6.4% | 3.86 | 73 | 22 | 15 | 12 | 18 |
| 2023 | 609 | 28.0% | 4.42 | 432 | 86 | 42 | 13 | 36 |
| 2024 | 444 | 20.4% | 4.46 | 349 | 32 | 15 | 16 | 32 |
| 2025 | 290 | 13.3% | 4.54 | 230 | 27 | 11 | 5 | 17 |
| 2026 | 146 | 6.7% | 4.61 | 116 | 16 | 5 | 5 | 4 |

**Note on the shape of this curve.** It is **flat-then-stepped**, not a decline: 3.86–4.09 every year from 2019 to 2022, then a step to 4.42 in 2023 and a steady climb to 4.61 in 2026. The step coincides with the May 2023 rating-prompt burst (Warning 3), and 2023 alone holds 28.0% of the corpus. **Interpretation: the 2023–2026 rise is partly a composition effect — more prompted, shorter, happier reviews — not purely a product improvement. Part 7 separates the two.**

## 1.5 Processing method

1. **Parse.** All 2,178 JSON lines parsed; schema verified field-by-field; HTML entities unescaped; records sorted by `date` ascending into a single flat working file (`temp/46-all-reviews.txt`).
2. **Reconcile.** Main file cross-checked against all 93 `by_country/*.jsonl` files and against `manifest.json` on totals, per-country counts and rating distribution. All checks exact (§1.3).
3. **Read.** Every record read individually and in full, in date order, in its original language (Warning 5). No sampling, no keyword pre-filtering, no truncation, no stopping at a context limit.
4. **Classify.** Themes hand-assigned per record while reading, recorded as an explicit `review_id → [themes]` map in `temp/46-review-classification.py`. Non-exclusive: a review carries every theme it supports.
5. **Validate.** Programmatic checks over the map: zero unknown review IDs, zero duplicate keys, zero unassigned records, zero records with no theme, zero duplicated themes within a record, zero undefined themes, zero orphan themes. All passed (§1.3).
6. **Aggregate.** Counts, percentages, means, rating cross-tabs, family unions, per-country cuts, per-era, per-half and per-year cuts computed from the validated map. Every number in this report is derived from that one artifact; the tables in §3.1, §3.2, §4.6, §9.1, §9.7 and §9.8 are generated from it directly rather than transcribed.
7. **Verify.** Every review ID cited anywhere in this report was checked to exist in `reviews.jsonl`; every §9.7 row was checked against the map and the source; every §3.1 count, percentage and mean was recomputed (§9.4).

## 1.6 Limitations and known biases

- **Solicited-burst contamination in 2023.** 297 records (Warning 3). Retained in denominators, flagged, and corrected for in trend work.
- **Selection bias toward writers.** Written reviews are a minority of ratings (roughly one in six on the US storefront). The corpus mean (4.332) is 0.37 below the displayed aggregate (4.7).
- **Contentless reviews.** 205 records (9.41%) carry no recoverable product content ("Good", "👍", "hao", keyboard-mash). They are retained and never used as evidence for a product theme.
- **Price and entitlement boundaries are review-derived.** The corpus shows what reviewers say is free or paid at the time they wrote. It cannot show exact release dates for pricing or entitlement changes; those are stated as inferences.
- **Version data is absent from the schema.** No `version` field exists on any record, so defect clusters are dated, not versioned. "After the update" attributions come from reviewer wording only.
- **Storefront ≠ language ≠ nationality.** Several US-storefront reviews are written in Spanish, Persian, Arabic, Korean, Chinese and French; storefront is used as the market unit throughout because it is the field the data provides.
- **Sub-1% themes.** 66 of the 109 themes have fewer than 22 supporting reviews (<1%). They are reported at their honest signal label and never promoted.

## 3.2 Theme-family aggregates

Each row is the **union** of its family's themes — a review carrying three reliability themes counts once. `mp_paid_subscription` and `mp_paid_lifetime` record that someone paid, not that they liked paying, so they sit in their own "Purchase evidence" family rather than inflating monetization praise.

| Family | Themes | Reviews (union) | % of 2,178 | Mean ★ | Share of the 250 1–2★ reviews | Signal |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Core praise | 21 | 1412 | 64.83% | 4.82 | 15/250 (6.0%) | **high-priority** |
| Monetization friction | 18 | 373 | 17.13% | 2.80 | 166/250 (66.4%) | **high-priority** |
| Meta | 3 | 250 | 11.48% | 4.57 | 16/250 (6.4%) | **high-priority** |
| Feature gaps & requests | 26 | 197 | 9.04% | 4.17 | 17/250 (6.8%) | **high-priority** |
| UX friction | 10 | 158 | 7.25% | 3.49 | 40/250 (16.0%) | **high-priority** |
| Reliability | 12 | 151 | 6.93% | 3.05 | 55/250 (22.0%) | **high-priority** |
| Monetization praise | 5 | 151 | 6.93% | 4.91 | 0/250 (0.0%) | **high-priority** |
| Segments | 5 | 108 | 4.96% | 4.75 | 5/250 (2.0%) | **very strong** |
| Purchase evidence | 2 | 89 | 4.09% | 4.12 | 17/250 (6.8%) | **very strong** |
| Support | 4 | 66 | 3.03% | 4.26 | 12/250 (4.8%) | **very strong** |
| Product-value criticism | 3 | 53 | 2.43% | 2.45 | 29/250 (11.6%) | meaningful |
| **Any praise** (core + monetization praise + support praise) | — | **1461** | **67.08%** | **4.82** | 15/250 (6.0%) | **high-priority** |

**Three structural facts follow from this table.**

**(a) Monetization friction is the negative pole, and it is 2.5× the size of monetization praise.** 373 reviews (17.13%, mean 2.80) against 151 (6.93%, mean 4.91). Report 43's one-time-purchase app had the opposite shape (praise 89 > friction 87). Here the pricing model is a net liability in the review record.

**(b) Monetization friction covers two-thirds of all bad reviews.** Of the 250 one- and two-star reviews, **166 (66.4%) carry a monetization-friction theme**, 55 (22.0%) a reliability theme, 40 (16.0%) a UX-friction theme and 29 (11.6%) a product-value criticism ("it's just a checkbox"). These overlap. Unlike report 43, where the monetization share was historical, **here it is live in every era** — 70% of 2018–2020 low-star reviews, 73% of 2021–early-2023, 51% of May–Dec 2023, 63% of 2024–2026 (§7).

**(c) Praise almost never coexists with a low rating.** Only 15 of 250 low-star reviews (6.0%) carry any core praise theme. When this product loses a reviewer, it is not because they disliked the grid; it is because they were stopped by the paywall, disagreed with the price, or lost data.

## 3.3 Positive themes in detail

### 3.3.1 Simplicity — 425 reviews (19.51%, high-priority, mean 4.87) — the top theme in the corpus, and still rising

Nearly one review in five praises minimalism, clarity or the absence of clutter, and the share *rises* in every era: **16.40% → 17.71% → 19.62% → 21.59%**. It is written in every language in the corpus. *"This app succeed where other streaks app fails for its simplicity and design. You have low over head, one click completion, and easy on the eyes"* (`3392010074`, US, 5★, 2018); *"Без лишних функций… То что нужно"* / "No unnecessary features… exactly what's needed" (`4306125740`, RU, 5★); *"I don't want to build a new habit of 'wasting time while tracking habits'"* (`8200705711`, CZ, 5★); *"Most habit trackers are themselves distracting, this one is simple"* (`10011917752`, US, 5★); *"I'm really sick of apps with a million features and AI just to convolute things"* (`13943168290`, US, 5★, Apr 2026); *"A lot of great apps start this way, then they start adding weird features that nobody wants… pls dont do that"* (`12631375398`, IN, 5★). Related: `cp_ease_of_use` (185, 8.49%, mean 4.87) — *"J'y passe littéralement 10 secondes par jour"* / "I spend literally 10 seconds a day on it" (`10907874412`, FR, 5★).

**The tension with finding 2 in the summary is direct.** The same word is used for and against the product: *"Simple and overpriced… for an app that is essentially a checkbox $30 (a year) is way too much"* (`7060353342`, US, 2★). **Interpretation: simplicity is the retention asset and the pricing liability at the same time. The fix is not to add complexity to justify the price; it is to change what the price is compared against (Part 8).**

### 3.3.2 Behaviour change — 283 reviews (12.99%, high-priority, mean 4.93)

These reviewers describe an outcome, not a feature: exercise, reading, language study, writing, meditation, medication, sobriety-adjacent routines, sleep. **Its mean of 4.93 is the joint-highest of any theme with n>50** (tied with `cp_generic_positive`). *"I started with 25 [words a day]. Now, thanks to the app, I've maintained a 40+ days streak, and I almost always write more than my goal (usually 300-1,500 words)"* (`9952583686`, US, 5★); *"me permitió encadenar hasta ahora cerca de 700 días!!"* / "it let me chain nearly 700 days so far!!" (`9942715625`, AR, 5★); *"With this app, a super lazy person managed to do over 1k pushups in 2025!"* (`13606203965`, US, 5★); *"I've already killed my social media addiction, become much more resistant to procrastination, got free from caffeine dependency"* (`11621928088`, GB, 5★); *"30 jours sont passés et j'ai effectivement créé l'habitude de faire un peu de Blender chaque jour… 62h d'expérience accumulés en 30 jours"* / "30 days on, I've built the habit of doing some Blender every day… 62 hours accumulated" (`14343402459`, FR, 5★). Bad-habit use is a distinct sub-theme: `cp_habit_breaking` (42, 1.93%, **mean 4.98**) — alcohol reduction (`10490353599`), added sugar (`9728139886`), smoking-adjacent and screen-time goals (`11657479158`).

### 3.3.3 The colour-saturation grid — 228 reviews (10.47%, high-priority, mean 4.86)

Analysed in summary finding 4. Three supporting themes describe the same mechanism from different angles: `cp_streak_motivation` (44, 2.02%, mean 4.80 — *"the pain of losing my streak is more painful than the exercise"* — `9516585789`), `cp_skip_forgiving` (41, 1.88%, mean 4.93 — *"you don't want to break a streak, so you keep going"* — `6549805388`; *"no 'punishment' other than a break in the color gradient"* — `6703493891`) and `cp_stats` (54, 2.48%, mean 4.91 — *"With this app I found out that I exercised 60% of the time, wrote 80% of the time, never went more than three days without flossing"* — `10483508663`). Two reviewers explicitly call it a GitHub contribution graph (`9567101427`, `10843646833`). **Against it:** 19 reviews (0.87%) find the triangle/half-square marks confusing (§3.4.4) and 4 say long streaks turn colours muddy.

### 3.3.4 Design and aesthetics — 227 reviews (10.42%, high-priority, mean 4.63)

The lowest-rated large praise theme, because it is often the *first clause* of a price complaint — 23 of 227 are 1–3★ (e.g. *"Beautiful app except the free version is useless"* — `5378982782`). Pure praise: *"eye candy matters… Color and graphics make it so much easier to assimilate data and Everyday positively EXCELS at this"* (`5280954299`, US, 5★); *"people who are looking at my screen with me always — always! — ask what the app is because the cascading colors look so cool"* (`9952382181`, US, 4★).

### 3.3.5 Competitive displacement — 198 reviews (9.09%, high-priority, mean 4.92)

One review in eleven says explicitly that they tried other habit trackers and chose this one. *"I have downloaded and tried nearly every habit tracker in the App Store all the way back to Chains.cc"* (`5352400235`, US, 5★); *"I've tried every habit building app available including Fabulous and Done. Those were either overly and unnecessarily complicated (and expensive) or too simple"* (`8871861613`, US, 5★); *"This is so much better than Streaks!!!! It's not even close"* (`12001038041`, AU, 5★); *"Basically it's habitica but without the stupid game mechanics"* (`9978469609`, PL, 5★); *"Я перепробовала каждое КАЖДОЕ ПРИЛОЖЕНИЕ по трекингу привычек и ЭТО САМОООЕ ЛУЧШЕЕ"* / "I tried EVERY habit app and THIS is the best" (`10949381982`, RU, 5★). Named alternatives across the corpus: Streaks, Productive, Fabulous, Done, Habitica, HabitShare, Habit, Finch, Momentum, Way of Life, mem:o, TickTick, (Not Boring) Habits, Awesome Habits, Habitty, Onrise, 习惯口袋, plus Apple Reminders, Excel/Google Sheets and paper. **Interpretation: the product wins on design-plus-simplicity against every named paid rival. It loses on price, and when it loses, reviewers mostly name a free or cheaper alternative (§5.7).**

### 3.3.6 Learn content and the founder's voice — 107 reviews (4.91%, very strong, mean 4.82) — but fading

The Learn tab, the 7-day email course and James Clear / Atomic Habits material are praised as a reason to stay: *"Content alone is like a life changing book. I didn't even start using the habit tracker in that app, and am already so amazed by the content and articles"* (`5355179743`, DE, 5★); *"the dev eats his own dogfood… surprisingly useful and inspirational content in the form of 'documentation'"* (`5280954299`); *"The picks are high quality, motivating, and have a time estimate for how long they'll consume"* (`10802899909`, US, 5★). **Its rate falls from 11.02% (2018–2020) to 3.30% (2024–2026)** — see §7.6. Five reviews say this content, and the emails, are English-only (`6821437095`, `6821741061`, `8985834449`, `9992608962`, `11171579515`).

### 3.3.7 Cross-platform, widgets, and the web — 72 + 66 + 23 reviews

`cp_cross_platform` (72, 3.31%, mean 4.82) and `cp_widget` (66, 3.03%, mean 4.79) are the two capability praises. Early users valued the **web app and Chrome new-tab extension** (`seg_web_or_extension_user`, 23): *"I have it as my default Chrome tab page so I am constantly getting reminded"* (`9779787899`, IN, 5★); *"I am not allowed my phone at work, at all. We can use computers that are hardwired"* (`8664752373`, US, 5★). Later users value the **widget**: *"The widget is exactly what I was looking for, I barely even open the app"* (`10864658754`, US, 5★); *"Не везде можно одним тыком с экрана домой отметить привычки"* / "Not every app lets you check habits with one tap from the home screen" (`13116954771`, RU, 5★). Widget praise rises from 0.81% to 3.98% across eras.

## 3.4 Negative themes in detail

### 3.4.1 The 3-habit free tier — 180 reviews (8.26%, high-priority, mean 2.43) — **live, unchanged, eight years**

| Year | Reviews | Cap reviews | Cap rate | 1★ reviews that year | …of which carry the cap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2019 | 86 | 10 | 11.6% | 14 | 4 (29%) |
| 2020 | 274 | 38 | 13.9% | 25 | 13 (52%) |
| 2021 | 177 | 28 | 15.8% | 19 | 11 (58%) |
| 2022 | 140 | 26 | 18.6% | 18 | 8 (44%) |
| 2023 | 609 | 32 | 5.3% | 36 | 10 (28%) |
| 2024 | 444 | 22 | 5.0% | 32 | 10 (31%) |
| 2025 | 290 | 18 | 6.2% | 17 | 11 (65%) |
| 2026 (to 4 Sep) | 146 | 6 | 4.1% | 4 | 1 (25%) |

**The rate fell in 2023; the count did not.** 26 reviews in 2022, 32 in 2023, 22 in 2024. The rate fell because the rating prompt tripled total volume with short positive reviews (Warning 3). **The cap itself never changed**: the listing, accessed 11 September 2026, still states it.

**It is the single biggest driver of one-star reviews: 68 of 166 (41.0%).** The objection has four recurring shapes:
- **Discovered after setup.** *"By the time you discover that you can only track 3 habits… you've already spent 20 minutes or more setting it up. Now you need to spend an equal amount of time removing all the stuff you installed"* (`8247232385`, US, 1★, 16 helpful votes); *"please consider it before spending the next 15 minutes to set up everything just to find out that this is useless unless you purchase a preminum"* (`8476763345`, VN, 3★).
- **Too small to evaluate.** `mf_trial_too_limited` (32, 1.47%, **mean 1.88**): *"Nelle condizioni odierne con sole 3 abitudini non è possibile testare l'app dovrei abbonarmi prima ancora di provarla"* / "With only 3 habits it's not possible to test the app — I'd have to subscribe before even trying it" (`6859946614`, IT, 1★); *"I'm always ready to buy a good app if I can try it for a while to test it… A better idea would be to give people the possibility to try it for 30 days without limitation"* (`9663261021`, US, 2★).
- **The listing says free.** `mf_misleading_free_claim` (27, 1.24%, **mean 1.85**, the lowest mean of any theme with n>20; `mf_trial_too_limited` at 1.88 is second): *"Potential but FREE FOREVER description is misleading"* (`5534964979`, US, 3★, 2020); *"The free version ('Everyday will be free forever!') is limited to just three ( = 3!!) items"* (`6856428999`, DE, 1★, 7 helpful votes); *"the developer's website claims this app will be 'free forever'… IS NOT A FREE APP, it just has a free try-it-and-see mode"* (`11692689432`, US, 2★, Sep 2024); *"Every picture [in the App Store] shows a bunch of colorful habit trackers, not a one shows what the free version looks like"* (`12132723234`, US, 4★, Jan 2025).
- **A modest counter-offer.** `mf_more_free_habits_request` (29, 1.33%, mean 3.69) — the reviewers who name a number: 4 (`9997347192`, `12505242555`, `11416426448`), 5 (`8334841669`, `11904537006`, `12134015424`, `13941461433`), 5–6 (`10900370698`), 6 (`9520604649`), 7 (`6163200202`). Nobody asks for unlimited. *"per la versione gratuita sarebbe cosa buona aumentare il numero di abitudini… da 3 a 6 almeno… inducendoli poi a fare un abbonamento premium nel momento in cui avranno simpatizzato per l'app"* / "raise the free tier from 3 to at least 6 so users get attached and then subscribe" (`9520604649`, IT, 3★).

**The widget defect.** The home-screen widget holds four rows. *"when I use the widget (which fits 4 habits) it leaves the 4th row blank because i can only have 3 and it bothers me so much"* (`9997347192`, US, 4★); *"you are limited to tracking only 3 habits. Which by the way makes many widgets appear broken as they tend to have a multiple of 4 rows"* (`12334331759`, BE, 1★). **Interpretation: the free tier is sized below the product's own display unit.**

**Two facts cut the other way and must be stated.** (1) **66 reviewers (3.03%, mean 4.92) say the free tier is enough** — *"only using the free version (so ltd to 3 habits). But 3 habits is a lot on some days!"* (`5368045613`, US, 5★); *"Da man sich ja auch nicht zu viel vornehmen sollte, komme ich bisher mit den drei freien Habits zurecht"* / "Since you shouldn't take on too much, I manage fine with the three free habits" (`13367736199`, DE, 5★); *"I think the limit is beneficial since it kept me from piling expectations onto myself"* (`13247499575`, US, 5★). (2) **15 of the 180 cap reviews are 5★** — people describing the cap neutrally. **Interpretation: the defensible version of the cap is the habit-science argument these reviewers make themselves. The indefensible version is discovering it after setup, or seeing it contradicted by the listing.**

### 3.4.2 Price and pricing model — 163 reviews (7.48%, high-priority, mean 2.84)

| Sub-theme | n | % | Mean ★ | Signal | Period |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `mf_price_too_high` | 163 | 7.48% | 2.84 | **high-priority** | 2019–2026 |
| `mf_want_one_time_purchase` | 53 | 2.43% | 3.32 | meaningful | 2019–2025 |
| `mf_anti_subscription` | 41 | 1.88% | 2.44 | meaningful | 2019–2026 |
| `mf_competitor_cheaper` | 28 | 1.29% | 2.25 | meaningful | 2019–2025 |
| `mf_lifetime_price_too_high` | 27 | 1.24% | 3.00 | meaningful | 2022–2026 |
| `mf_regional_pricing` | 8 | 0.37% | 3.50 | weak | 2020–2026 |
| `mf_paywalled_capability` | 8 | 0.37% | 2.12 | weak | 2020–2025 |
| `mf_discount_request` | 6 | 0.28% | 3.67 | weak | 2021–2026 |
| `mf_price_increase` | 5 | 0.23% | 2.20 | weak | 2020–2025 |
| `mf_longevity_doubt` | 5 | 0.23% | 4.20 | weak | 2019–2025 |
| `mf_no_family_sharing` | 2 | 0.09% | 2.00 | ignore by count; stated because it is a clear product gap | 2023–2024 |

**The subscription objection is about renting, not about paying.** *"Pero una cosa es COMPRAR un producto y otra ALQUILAR… Ponme un precio fijo establecido con un periodo de prueba inicial para probar la app, no una suscripción y me tienes"* / "Buying a product is one thing, renting it is another… Give me a fixed price with an initial trial, not a subscription, and you've got me" (`6119755511`, ES, 2★); *"Ich kaufe gerne eine App aber, ich hasse es ständig gemolken zu werden"* / "I'm happy to buy an app, but I hate being milked constantly" (`6258595511`, DE, 4★); *"I'm willing to pay for it, but I'm not willing to subscribe to it forever"* (`8309866369`, US, 1★). The ask moved from "sell it once" to "sell it once for less" once the lifetime tier appeared (summary finding 3, §7.3).

**The value comparison.** `neg_value_scepticism` (32, 1.47%, **mean 2.16**) — the reviewers who argue the product does nothing a spreadsheet, a notebook or Apple Reminders cannot: *"You click squares and colours appear… 'college project'"* (`5382735835`, PT, 1★); *"una checklist con esteroides"* / "a checklist on steroids" (`6391262502`, ES, 2★); *"$5 a month for a spreadsheet. No thanks"* (`8919460787`, US, 1★); *"The app doesn't actually DO anything… The app would need to actually complete the tasks for me to make the $30 subscription worth it"* (`9466251748`, US, 1★). A smaller cluster says the app itself has stood still: `neg_stagnant_development` (5, mean 1.60) — *"This is… just quite minimal, basic and finished app on life-support… identical to how it was like 5+ years ago"* (`13689294667`, KZ, 2★, Jan 2026); *"从去年开始1.9.19 到 2.4.1，我没看出有什么新变化"* / "From 1.9.19 last year to 2.4.1, I see no changes" (`7830233665`, CN, 1★). Against this, reviewers who have used it for years say the opposite: *"This app gets better and better overtime"* (`13380357043`, US, 5★); *"there are small updates periodically that makes sense but don't overwhelm"* (`13749734782`, US, 5★).

**Paywalled capabilities other than habit count** (8, 0.37%, mean 2.12): **dark mode** (`6139789378`, `11054384115`, `11382464605` — *"New update moved 'dark mode' behind a paywall. Immediate deletion"*) and **offline use** (`10143971103`, `10618466787`, `13460787737`, `10448268225`). **Interpretation: gating dark mode is a small-revenue, high-irritation choice; it shows up in 1★ and 2★ reviews at the exact moment it changes.**

### 3.4.3 Reliability — 151 reviews (6.93%, high-priority, mean 3.05) across twelve sub-themes

| Sub-theme | n | % of 2,178 | Mean ★ | Signal | Period |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `rel_crash_freeze` | 25 | 1.15% | 3.04 | meaningful | 2019–2024 |
| `rel_data_loss` | 24 | 1.10% | 2.12 | meaningful | 2020–2026 |
| `rel_misc_bug` | 23 | 1.06% | 3.17 | meaningful | 2019–2026 |
| `rel_widget_broken` | 21 | 0.96% | 3.33 | emerging | 2021–2025 |
| `rel_offline_mode_bug` | 17 | 0.78% | 3.53 | emerging | 2019–2023 |
| `rel_sync_issues` | 15 | 0.69% | 3.13 | emerging | 2020–2026 |
| `rel_layout_scroll_bug` | 13 | 0.60% | 3.38 | emerging | 2019–2025 |
| `rel_login_signup_fail` | 9 | 0.41% | 1.89 | weak | 2018–2024 |
| `rel_mac_performance` | 9 | 0.41% | 3.78 | weak | 2020–2025 |
| `rel_notifications_broken` | 7 | 0.32% | 3.14 | weak | 2019–2023 |
| `rel_offline_use_blocked` | 7 | 0.32% | 2.14 | weak | 2021–2025 |
| `rel_accidental_tap` | 3 | 0.14% | 3.67 | weak | 2023–2026 |

Reliability is a smaller family here than in report 43 (6.93% vs 11.60%) and its *rate* falls over time (12.63% → 10.22% → 4.38% → 4.55%). **But its composition worsened**: the early defects were outages; the recent ones are silent corruption of the record.

**Early era — outages that recovered.** Two tight clusters:
- **China "offline mode" (Sept 2019 – Apr 2020).** 14 of 17 `rel_offline_mode_bug` reviews fall in 2019–2020 and 16 of 17 are Chinese. Nine arrive in the nine days 20–28 September 2019: `4808959996` (*"为什么总说我连不上网？明明连着的呀"* / "Why does it keep saying I'm offline? I'm clearly connected"), `4810179733`, `4812186865`, `4834666537` (*"网络连接问题仍没有修复好"* / "The network problem still isn't fixed"), `4835179226`, `4836234866`, `4839785600`, `4842624645`, `4848868262` (*"根本用不了"* / "Completely unusable"). A second wave follows in March–April 2020 (`5612974809`, `5624585393`, `5699845268`, `5766390109` — on iPad version 1.9.19 — and `5770490572`). Only two later instances exist (`9917968507` CN, May 2023; `10451430334` CN, Oct 2023) plus one Brazilian (`9744876901`).
- **"Won't open after the update" (16–18 September 2020).** Six reviews in three days from five storefronts: `6436513011` (GB), `6436882098` (US, *"I can't even open the app anymore ever since I updated it!!"*), `6437368606` (GB, iPhone 7), `6437984231` (US), `6438265463` (UY, *"iOS 13.7"*), `6440260322` (CN, *"疯狂闪退！"* / "Crashing like crazy!"). **Interpretation, labelled: the cluster coincides with the iOS 14 release window; the corpus cannot say whether the cause was the OS or the app update. It did not recur at that scale.** A June 2020 freeze cluster (`6045093199`, `6054354621`, `6064279767` and a statistics-screen crash `6057607224`) is smaller and also resolved.

**Recent era — the record itself becomes unreliable.** `rel_data_loss` (24, 1.10%, mean 2.12; analysed in summary finding 6) grows from 0.73% to 1.47% across halves. Two related themes describe the same failure through a different surface:
- `rel_widget_broken` (21, 0.96%): widgets that are blank, stale, or show wrong data — *"After January ended I deleted the Dry January task but the widget still shows it"* (`12294579474`, US, 4★); *"每天都会发生主屏幕小组件色块混乱的情况… 但隔一段时间色块自动消失"* / "every day the home-screen widget colour blocks get scrambled… after a while they vanish" (`12473456800`, CN, 4★); *"The widget is the heart of the application"* (`11527532536`, PE, 1★, still failing after contact with the developer).
- `rel_sync_issues` (15, 0.69%): phone ↔ Watch ↔ Mac disagreement — *"finished goals on the watch won't show up on the phone and vice versa… since the watch is the main place i would want to keep track of things this is a deal breaker"* (`10277533659`, US, 2★); *"The Mac app is slow and clunky. Does not sync with my phone app in a timely manner. I paid a hefty price to sync with all my devices"* (`13459643027`, US, 2★, subscriber).

`rel_accidental_tap` (3) completes the picture: an interactive widget that records taps nobody meant — *"If touched accidentally it marks the completion of that day by mistake as well. Not great for a paid app"* (`10386394785`, IN, 1★, subscriber); *"Сделайте отдельно режим просмотра и режим редактирования для табло"* / "Make separate view and edit modes for the board, so there are no accidental taps when scrolling" (`13783066497`, RU, 5★). **The data-integrity union (data loss ∪ sync ∪ widget broken ∪ accidental tap) is 56 reviews (2.57%, meaningful, mean 2.91), rising from 1.08% (2018–2020) to 2.95% (2024–2026).**

**Login and account failures** (`rel_login_signup_fail`, 9, **mean 1.89**) are concentrated in the account-required era: the very first review in the corpus (`3038410981`, 2018), `5051054298` (CN, *"注册转了半个小时"* / "registration spun for half an hour"), `8409233319` (CA, 1★, password reset email never arrives), `10771638093` (IN, Sign in with Apple loop), `10988898467` (CA, 1★, account not recognised, data gone).

### 3.4.4 UX friction — 158 reviews (7.25%, high-priority, mean 3.49)

| Sub-theme | n | % | Mean ★ | Signal |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ux_review_prompt_nag` | 50 | 2.30% | 3.22 | meaningful |
| `ux_design_dated_or_clunky` | 34 | 1.56% | 3.56 | meaningful |
| `ux_onboarding_confusion` | 19 | 0.87% | 3.95 | emerging |
| `ux_symbols_confusing` | 19 | 0.87% | 3.79 | emerging |
| `ux_localization_gap` | 13 | 0.60% | 4.08 | emerging |
| `ux_cannot_reorder` | 10 | 0.46% | 4.10 | weak |
| `ux_history_view_limited` | 9 | 0.41% | 3.44 | weak |
| `ux_colour_gets_muddy` | 4 | 0.18% | 3.50 | weak |
| `ux_upsell_prompt_nag` | 4 | 0.18% | 1.50 | weak |
| `ux_email_spam` | 3 | 0.14% | 1.67 | weak |

**`ux_review_prompt_nag` (50)** is analysed in summary finding 7. It is **62% US (31/50)** against a 33.4% baseline, 28 of 50 are 1–3★, and the complaints are specific about the mechanism: no "don't ask again" (`12129865860`), asking during first-run setup (`10056037504`, *"It's asking whether I like the app just as I downloaded it!"*; `10053587799`, *"every other time I marked a habit as complete the app popped up a window asking me to rate it"*), asking payers (`9982660905`, AU, *"Nice app and I'm a subscriber but it continually asks you to provide reviews"*), asking across platforms (`9995971461`, *"I already submitted a feedback but it kept on asking across all platform!"*). Its companion **`ux_upsell_prompt_nag`** (4, **mean 1.50**) is the paywall equivalent: *"everytiime I try to add a new habit or move between screens the 'upgrade to premium' screen appears and has to be 'x' multiple times"* (`10762802152`, US, 1★).

**`ux_symbols_confusing` (19)** — the half-square and triangle are the colour system's weakest point: *"颜色只有半格是什么意思啊？"* / "What does a half-filled square mean?" (`4850070969`, CN, 2019) and *"半个格到底是什么意思啊，没看见介绍再也找不着了"* / "What does the half square actually mean? I missed the intro and can't find it again" (`4869150597`, CN); *"What does the triangle and squares mean?????"* (`7384017889`, US, 1★); *"Why would they make me have to put a half triangle for a habit that is only supposed to be done weekly? So demoralizing… I signed up for the yearly but will cancel until this is fixed"* (`10257601209`, US, 2★, subscriber); *"Nu prea înțeleg rostul acelor triunghiuri"* / "I don't really understand the point of those triangles" (`13762762742`, RO, 4★, Feb 2026). **Interpretation: seven years of the same question, from the first-run screen onward — a one-screen legend fixes it.**

**`ux_design_dated_or_clunky` (34)** — non-native controls and a mobile layout on desktop: *"It really looks like they have tried to shoehorn the webview into a smaller screen"* (`3642699474`, GB, 1★, 2019); *"UI가 반쯤 네이티브하다고 느껴져서… Desktop UI가 Mobile UI에 지나치게 영향을 많이 받아서"* / "The UI feels half-native… the desktop UI is too heavily shaped by the mobile UI" (`10906751585`, KR, 5★); *"no sigue ningún patrón de diseño esperado en iOS"* / "follows none of the expected iOS design patterns" (`10932557854`, MX, 4★). One specific regression: the December 2022 completion animation (*"please remove the new animation whenever u log a day it's so annoying"* — `9443080997`; *"'Special FX' update unnecessary… I'll not renew"* — `9462122321`, US, 2★, subscriber).

**`ux_localization_gap` (13)** — the UI is now in 15 languages (§2.3), so the remaining gap is the **content**: the Learn tab, videos and emails. *"Lo malo es que todo el 'soporte': consejos, ayuda, videos, reto de 7 días... está en inglés, para mi sorpresa, pues el desarrollador es español. Si estuviera en español, me plantearía suscribirme"* / "The bad part is that all the 'support' — tips, help, videos, 7-day challenge — is in English, which surprised me because the developer is Spanish. If it were in Spanish, I'd consider subscribing" (`8985834449`, ES, 4★); *"ينقصه أمر واحد فقط.. وهو ترجمة الكتب والمقاطع التعليمية"* / "It lacks only one thing — translating the books and instructional videos" (`11171579515`, KW, 4★). Earlier UI requests (Turkish, `6164086392`, 16 helpful votes, Jul 2020; Chinese, `5999679530`, `7999081675`) appear to have been answered — Turkish is praised by December 2022 (`9410319036`, *"Türkçe olarak bir araya getirilmiş"* / "brought together in Turkish") and both Chinese scripts are in today's listing.

**`ux_email_spam` (3, mean 1.67)** is early and resolved: *"每天十二点准时发邮件真的很打扰人，还不知道怎么取消"* / "An email at exactly 12 every day is really intrusive, and I can't find how to cancel" (`5910868374`, CN); *"the sender has a new address for each message"* (`6200581271`, FI, 1★). By 2021 a reviewer notes *"You can turn them off if you don't like it"* (`7128048673`).

| Sub-theme | n | % | Mean ★ | Signal | Period |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sup_responsive_praise` | 53 | 2.43% | 4.94 | meaningful | 2019–2026 |
| `sup_unresponsive` | 7 | 0.32% | 1.57 | weak | 2022–2026 |

Analysed in summary finding 9. The unresponsive seven: `8285529014` (DE, 3★, *"Leider keine zeitnahe Antwort auf Anfrage wegen eines Fehlers"* / "Unfortunately no timely answer to a bug report"), `8409233319` (CA, 1★), `9525145329` (DE, 2★, *"no reaction to good suggestions"*), `11089487551` (CH, 1★, *"sent 3 different email… Not a single reply"*), `13298363427` (IN, 1★), `13765557194` (RU, 2★, paid, unresolved data loss), `14423487007` (US, 1★, paid, unrecognised subscription). **Four of the seven are 2024–2026 and two are paying customers.**

**Three observations.** First, **the feature-gap union (197, 9.04%) has a mean of 4.17** — these are requests from people who like the product; only 17 of 197 (8.6%) are 1★ or 2★. Second, **four requests have visibly shipped during the corpus** — widgets (2020), Apple Watch (2021), journal and calendar (Dec 2023), folders (2025) — and in each case the request theme stops and a praise theme starts (§7.7). Third, **the two largest open requests — colours and scheduling — are also the two that sit closest to the colour grid itself**, which is the product's differentiator.

---

# PART 4 — RATINGS ANALYSIS

Distribution: **5★ 1,532 (70.34%) · 4★ 253 (11.62%) · 3★ 143 (6.57%) · 2★ 84 (3.86%) · 1★ 166 (7.62%)** · mean **4.332**.

The shape is strongly top-heavy, with a thin but distinct negative tail. 81.96% of reviews are 4–5★. The 4★ and 3★ bands are small (18.2% together) but they are where the corpus explains what is missing.

## 4.1 Five stars — n = 1,532 (70.34%)

| Theme | n | % of 5★ |
|---|---|---|
| `cp_simplicity` | 381 | 24.9% |
| `cp_behaviour_change` | 269 | 17.6% |
| `cp_visual_colour_system` | 200 | 13.1% |
| `cp_competitive_displacement` | 185 | 12.1% |
| `cp_beautiful_design` | 180 | 11.7% |
| `meta_low_information` | 169 | 11.0% |
| `cp_ease_of_use` | 168 | 11.0% |
| `cp_generic_positive` | 165 | 10.8% |
| `cp_learn_content` | 92 | 6.0% |
| `cp_cross_platform` | 64 | 4.2% |
| `mp_free_tier_sufficient` | 61 | 4.0% |
| `cp_widget` | 57 | 3.7% |
| `sup_responsive_praise` | 50 | 3.3% |
| `cp_stats` | 49 | 3.2% |

**Why people give 5★:** it is simple, it looks good, the colours pull them back, and it changed a behaviour. Those four appear together constantly. **Caveats on this band:** 219 of the 1,532 are from the May–June 2023 burst and 169 carry `meta_low_information` ("Good", "👍", "hao"). Stripping contentless reviews leaves **1,363 substantive 5★ reviews (62.6% of the corpus)**. Twenty 5★ reviews are rating/text contradictions (§9.3) — most report that the app will not open or connect (`4836234866`, `4839785600`, `6436882098`, `6437984231`, `6440260322`, `8993455225`, `9917968507`) or protest the rating prompt (`9941031361`, `9955874108`, `10007869022`).

## 4.2 Four stars — n = 253 (11.62%)

| Theme | n | % of 4★ |
|---|---|---|
| `cp_simplicity` | 33 | 13.0% |
| `mf_free_cap_3habits` | 33 | 13.0% |
| `mf_price_too_high` | 30 | 11.9% |
| `meta_low_information` | 27 | 10.7% |
| `cp_beautiful_design` | 24 | 9.5% |
| `cp_visual_colour_system` | 23 | 9.1% |
| `fr_more_colours` | 15 | 5.9% |
| `mf_want_one_time_purchase` | 14 | 5.5% |
| `fr_flexible_frequency` | 12 | 4.7% |
| `mf_more_free_habits_request` | 11 | 4.3% |

**The 4★ band is where the price of the fifth star is named.** *"Only reason I gave it 4 starts is because it would be so great to have a share/compete with friends"* (`6640963989`, US); *"minus 1 star for me because it's kind of upsetting that you can only add 3 habits before having to pay"* (`9997347192`, US); *"the only reason I'm not leaving a five star review is because I feel like the App Store presentation of the app is misleading"* (`12132723234`, US); *"5 Sterne gibt es jedoch nicht, da ich die kostenlose Demo-Version getestet habe… Jedoch kann ich sie nicht löschen"* / "No 5 stars, because after the trial I can't delete the extra habits — they're locked" (`8681575995`, DE). China contributes 37 of the 253 four-star reviews (14.6% of the band against 6.2% of the corpus) — CN reviewers disproportionately rate 4★ while naming the price or the offline bug (`4808959996`, `5624585393`, `5753851503`, `6563915292`). **Interpretation: 4★ here is "I love it but…", and the "but" is split roughly evenly between monetization (cap, price, one-time) and colour/scheduling requests.**

## 4.3 Three stars — n = 143 (6.57%)

| Theme | n | % of 3★ |
|---|---|---|
| `mf_free_cap_3habits` | 35 | 24.5% |
| `mf_price_too_high` | 32 | 22.4% |
| `cp_beautiful_design` | 13 | 9.1% |
| `mf_want_one_time_purchase` | 11 | 7.7% |
| `ux_review_prompt_nag` | 11 | 7.7% |
| `cp_simplicity` | 10 | 7.0% |
| `mf_anti_subscription` / `mp_paid_subscription` / `mf_lifetime_price_too_high` / `mf_trial_too_limited` / `mf_more_free_habits_request` / `neg_value_scepticism` | 7–8 each | 4.9–5.6% |

**Three stars is the "capped satisfaction" band, and the cap is literal.** *"Serían 5 Estrellas si la versión free te deja poner mas de 3 hábitos"* / "It would be 5 stars if the free version let you add more than 3 habits" (`10103326762`, AR); *"3 stars for the limitations of the free version"* (`10022293641`, GB); *"Talvez desse cinco estrelas com o uso, mas por causa da insistência me cobrando por uma avaliação… são apenas 3 estrelas mesmo"* / "I might have given five stars with use, but because of the insistence on a rating… it's only 3" (`10892779579`, BR); *"Оценка изменится, когда будет горизонтальный режим"* / "The rating will change when there's a landscape mode" (`8971421965`, GE). **11 of 143 (7.7%) are review-prompt complaints — the prompt is actively capping ratings it was designed to raise.**

## 4.4 Two stars — n = 84 (3.86%)

| Theme | n | % of 2★ |
|---|---|---|
| `mf_free_cap_3habits` | 29 | 34.5% |
| `mf_price_too_high` | 26 | 31.0% |
| `mf_trial_too_limited` | 8 | 9.5% |
| `mp_paid_subscription` | 8 | 9.5% |
| `ux_review_prompt_nag` / `mf_competitor_cheaper` / `neg_value_scepticism` / `mf_lifetime_price_too_high` | 7 each | 8.3% |
| `rel_data_loss` / `rel_sync_issues` / `mf_anti_subscription` | 6 each | 7.1% |

**Two stars is the paywall band plus the unhappy-subscriber band.** 10 of 84 (11.9%) are payers — a higher payer density than any other band — and their complaints are sync (`11016476901` Watch, `13459643027` Mac), data loss (`12356330098`, `13741293681`, `13765557194`), the skip symbols (`10257601209`), an unwanted animation (`9462122321`), a widget missing after paying (`7422190770`), reminders that could not be set after paying (`4864820069`) and an annual plan the reviewer wanted refunded (`6593564259`).

## 4.5 One star — n = 166 (7.62%)

| Theme | n | % of 1★ |
|---|---|---|
| `mf_free_cap_3habits` | 68 | 41.0% |
| `mf_price_too_high` | 45 | 27.1% |
| `mf_anti_subscription` | 16 | 9.6% |
| `mf_trial_too_limited` | 15 | 9.0% |
| `mf_misleading_free_claim` | 14 | 8.4% |
| `neg_value_scepticism` | 13 | 7.8% |
| `mf_competitor_cheaper` | 12 | 7.2% |
| `mf_want_one_time_purchase` | 11 | 6.6% |
| `ux_review_prompt_nag` / `rel_data_loss` | 10 each | 6.0% |
| `rel_crash_freeze` / `meta_rating_text_mismatch` | 8 each | 4.8% |

**Unlike report 43, the one-star band did not change character over time.** The share of 1–2★ reviews carrying a monetization-friction theme is **70% (2018–2020), 73% (2021 – Apr 2023), 51% (May–Dec 2023), 63% (2024–2026)**. What changed is the tail: the share carrying a data-integrity theme rises **5% → 8% → 11% → 15%**, and the share carrying UX friction (mostly the rating prompt) rises **7% → 13% → 31% → 19%**. **Interpretation: a 2026 one-star review of this app is still most likely to be about the paywall — but it is now twice as likely as in 2020 to be about lost data or the rating prompt.**

**12 of the 166 one-star reviews (7.2%) are from payers** — `5138933764` (RU, paid, crashes on iOS 9 iPad mini), `6863161594` (TW, premium not recognised on iPhone), `6866992048` (US, trial not recognised), `6998451648` (CA, $40 annual, history "vanishes", moved to Done), `10386394785` (IN, widget stale, accidental taps), `10514724470` (US, squares erased), `10951679021` (US, lifetime, keeps resetting), `11022906761` (US, charged on day one of the trial), `11748480154` (US, all data lost, refund), `12285802750` (US, charged immediately), `13416396839` (ID, results reassigned between habits), `14423487007` (US, subscription not recognised, no reply). **Every one of the twelve is a delivery or billing failure; none objects to the product's design.**
