| `mf_price_too_high` | 163 | 30 | 30 | 32 | 26 | 45 | 2.84 |
| `cp_learn_content` | 107 | 92 | 11 | 4 | 0 | 0 | 4.82 |
| `mp_paid_subscription` | 74 | 47 | 5 | 7 | 8 | 7 | 4.04 |
| `cp_cross_platform` | 72 | 64 | 5 | 2 | 0 | 1 | 4.82 |
| `cp_widget` | 66 | 57 | 6 | 2 | 0 | 1 | 4.79 |
| `mp_free_tier_sufficient` | 66 | 61 | 5 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 4.92 |
| `fr_more_colours` | 59 | 35 | 15 | 5 | 3 | 1 | 4.36 |
| `cp_stats` | 54 | 49 | 5 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 4.91 |
| `mf_want_one_time_purchase` | 53 | 14 | 14 | 11 | 3 | 11 | 3.32 |
| `sup_responsive_praise` | 53 | 50 | 3 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 4.94 |
| `mp_worth_the_money` | 51 | 48 | 3 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 4.94 |

**Four themes are genuinely non-exclusive across the rating range and should not be read as one-directional:**
- **`mf_free_cap_3habits`** appears at 5★ fifteen times — people describing the cap neutrally or endorsing it (*"It let's me track 3 habit for free and really more than that is too much to start all at once anyway!"* — `5284889566`).
- **`mf_price_too_high`** appears at 5★ thirty times, typically *"I love it but it's expensive"* — `6451462235` (CN, *"挺好用的，就是有点贵"* / "Very useful, just a bit expensive"), `10897862527` (GB, *"quite expensive but I suppose that's the price you have to pay"*).
- **`cp_beautiful_design`** appears at 1★ five times and 2★ five times, always as the opening clause of a paywall complaint.
- **`meta_low_information`** is 82% 5★ (169 of 205) and 41% of it sits in the 2023 rating-prompt era — contentless reviews in this corpus are overwhelmingly prompted praise.

---

# PART 5 — PAID-USER AND UPGRADE ANALYSIS

## 5.1 Who is identifiable as a payer

**97 reviewers (4.45% of 2,178, very strong) give first-person evidence of having paid** — 74 subscribers (`mp_paid_subscription`), 16 lifetime buyers (`mp_paid_lifetime`), and 13 who report being charged (`mf_billing_problem`), with overlap. The rule is deliberately conservative: "I paid", "I subscribed", "I bought the lifetime", "I'm a subscriber", naming a price paid, or reporting a charge. Recommending that others pay, or "I will buy it", does **not** count.

| Metric | Payers (n=97) | Non-payers (n=2,081) |
|---|---|---|
| Mean rating | **3.95** | **4.35** |
| 5★ | 60 (61.9%) | 1,472 (70.7%) |
| 4★ | 6 (6.2%) | 247 (11.9%) |
| 3★ | 9 (9.3%) | 134 (6.4%) |
| 2★ | 10 (10.3%) | 74 (3.6%) |
| 1★ | 12 (12.4%) | 154 (7.4%) |

**Payers are 1.7× as likely as non-payers to leave a one-star review, and 2.9× as likely to leave a two-star review.** The payer rate is higher on the US storefront (44 of 728, 6.04%) than elsewhere (53 of 1,450, 3.66%). **Treat the storefront difference as a weak signal**; it is consistent with, but does not prove, a price-sensitivity gap (§6).

By era: 14 payers in 2018–2020 (3.76% of that era), 26 in 2021 – Apr 2023 (6.48%), 19 in May–Dec 2023 (3.62%), 38 in 2024–2026 (4.32%). Lifetime buyers appear only from May 2021 and 10 of the 16 are 2024–2026. The full payer list is in §9.3.

## 5.2 What triggers a purchase — in buyers' own words

Five distinct triggers appear:

1. **The trial worked.** *"I paid for the full version after the trial because I could see how much it had helped in just a week"* (`9959381704`, GB, 5★); *"Absolutely did not hesitate to purchase after the week trial"* (`11333501919`, US, 5★); *"I tried it and a week later paid the anual subscription. It is worth it"* (`14285579446`, ES, 5★); *"I have used this app for 6 days and fully expected to delete it before the free trial ended. However… I will be subscribing for the year if not more"* (`9486286103`, CA, 5★ — tracking medication to share with a health-care team); *"Started with no subscription but after two weeks it was working so well for me that I did the yearly subscription"* (`11397647494`, US, 5★). *"j'ai voulu attendre une semaine avant de m'abonner pour être sûr de la qualité"* / "I waited a week before subscribing to be sure of the quality" (`4861726026`, FR, 5★, 2019).
2. **A fourth habit.** *"The 3 free habits wasn't enough for me. so I've paid £30 for the year"* (`6832692292`, GB, 5★); *"Habe mir die Premiumversion geholt, weil ich mehr als 3 habits eintragen möchte… Das sind gerade mal 2,50 im Monat"* / "I got premium because I want more than 3 habits… it's just 2.50 a month" (`7258835816`, DE, 5★); *"I've been using the free version of this app for a couple of months to track medication management… I went ahead with the upgrade today so that I could track other habits as well"* (`12573082987`, US, 5★).
3. **Cross-device access.** *"I did upgrade so the data could be shown and updated on my watch"* (`9822343856`, US, 3★); *"this has worked great between my windows desktop, iPad, and iPhone… Signed up for a year and it was worth every dollar"* (`5233752821`, US, 5★); *"For someone who loves data, this app is totally worth the yearly subscription… The web interface is great too"* (`9951354980`, US, 5★).
4. **Supporting an independent developer.** `mp_support_indie` (13, 0.60%, mean 4.92) — *"I paid the subscription fee because it's a great app with a developer who obviously cares"* (`4997111034`, US, 5★, ADHD); *"Koupil jsem si abych podpořil vývojáře"* / "I bought it to support the developer" (`11246800859`, CZ, 4★). **12 of the 13 are dated before July 2023** (§7.6).
5. **A lifetime option finally existing.** `12237402875` (CN, quoted in summary finding 3); *"I had the app for one year on the annual plan, and after missing it for a couple of months, I bought the lifetime plan"* (`9980580786`, CA, 5★); *"Mag keine Abomodelle und habe den Lifetime-Kauf gewählt. Klar, ist teuer, aber ich unterstütze gerne gute Umsetzungen"* / "I don't like subscriptions so I chose lifetime. Sure, it's expensive, but I'm glad to support good work" (`12695851528`, DE, 5★).

Two promotional triggers also appear: a Black Friday price (`6694266981`, BE, 3★, *"the Black Friday deal (which right now is 15 euro)"*) and a discount honoured after it expired (`5959721651`, US, 5★, *"I had previously seen a discount in December and I must have missed it, but Joan still hooked me up with the discounted subscription"*).

**Stated purchase intent (unthemed; illustrative):** several free users state a condition for paying — *"如果成功帮助养成三个习惯，就来付费使用！"* / "If it helps me build three habits, I'll come back and pay!" (`13535076908`, CN, 5★); *"I am really close to paying for it so I can add a 4th habit"* (`12197567600`, US, 5★, nearly a year of use); *"I'm about to pay for the lifetime membership"* (`13390939473`, US, 5★). The corpus cannot say whether they converted.

## 5.3 What paid users value after buying

Payers at 5★ (60 of 97) cite the same drivers as everyone else — colour system (15 payers), competitive displacement (14), worth the money (14), simplicity (13), behaviour change (11). What is distinctive is **longevity**: 6 payers are also long-time users (`9183960986`, CN, *"订阅两年了，最爱用的习惯追踪app"* / "Subscribed for two years — my favourite habit tracker"; `9945464712`, US, *"Will renew my annual subscription at the end of the month"*; `13973660306`, IN, *"Paid customer for several years now"*). Price reasonableness is rare among payers (2 of 97) — **payers justify the price by outcome, not by comparison**: *"Best ROI on your money of pretty much anything in life I can think of"* (`12116178936`, US, 5★); *"$29/year for what this app is giving me in terms of mental health is an absolute steal"* (`6703493891`, US, 5★).

## 5.4 Post-purchase failures — where paying customers get hurt

| Failure mode | Payers affected | Segment rate (n=97) | Global | IDs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Any reliability defect after paying** | **21** | **21.6%** | 0.96% | `4864820069` `5138933764` `6269178985` `6832692292` `6863161594` `6998451648` `7422190770` `9059567695` `9445697089` `9822343856` `10036343519` `10386394785` `10514724470` `10951679021` `11016476901` `11748480154` `12356330098` `13416396839` `13459643027` `13741293681` `13765557194` |
| **Billing failure** | **13** | **13.4%** | 0.60% | `6593564259` `6863161594` `6866992048` `9059567695` `9977790318` `10373652027` `11022906761` `11462388063` `11748480154` `12285802750` `13765557194` `14054333465` `14423487007` |
| Data loss | 8 | 8.2% | 0.37% | `6998451648` `10514724470` `10951679021` `11748480154` `12356330098` `13416396839` `13741293681` `13765557194` |
| Sync failure (phone/Watch/Mac/iPad) | 6 | 6.2% | 0.28% | `6863161594` `9822343856` `11016476901` `13459643027` `13741293681` `13765557194` |
| Subscription / trial not recognised | 4 | 4.1% | 0.18% | `6863161594` `6866992048` `11462388063` `14423487007` |
| Refund sought or given | 4 | 4.1% | 0.18% | `6593564259` `11462388063` (refunded) `11748480154` `13765557194` (refused) |
| Widget broken after paying | 3 | 3.1% | 0.14% | `7422190770` `10386394785` `12356330098` |
| Crash / won't open | 3 | 3.1% | 0.14% | `5138933764` `9059567695` `9445697089` |
| Charged on day one of a "free trial" | 2 | 2.1% | 0.09% | `11022906761` `12285802750` |
| Charged the wrong amount | 1 | 1.0% | 0.05% | `10373652027` (CHF 40.45 instead of 29.95) |
| Support did not answer | 2 | 2.1% | 0.09% | `13765557194` `14423487007` |

**The subscription-recognition cluster is the most damaging single pattern**, because it fails at the moment of purchase: *"Subscribed to free trial, have email confirmation from Apple. App does not recognize it and won't let me add more than three habits, even after refreshing the subscription with the provided link. On to the next app..."* (`6866992048`, US, 1★); *"明明都在pad己開通高級版，Iphone上卻不給用"* / "I clearly activated premium on my iPad, but my iPhone won't let me use it" (`6863161594`, TW, 1★); *"Оплатила подписку на год, деньги списали, но премиум аккаунт не подключился… деньги вернулись. Оплатила заново — все работает!"* / "Paid for a year, money taken, premium not activated… money returned. Paid again — everything works!" (`11462388063`, RU, 5★ — the one resolved case). **Interpretation: entitlement sync across devices and accounts is fragile, and when it breaks it produces the lowest-rated reviews a payer can write.**

## 5.5 Refunds, cancellations and billing

**4 reviews (0.18%, weak) seek or reference a refund** — against 2 of 638 in report 43's one-time-purchase corpus:
- `6593564259` (CA, 2★, Oct 2020): *"Not worth the $$ I wish I could get a refund for my annual purchase."*
- `11748480154` (US, 1★, Sep 2024): *"Lost all data!!! How do I get a refund?"*
- `13765557194` (RU, 2★, Feb 2026): *"деньги тоже никто не собирался возвращать за некачественный продукт"* / "nobody was going to refund the money for a defective product."
- `11462388063` (RU, 5★, Jul 2024): refunded after a failed activation, then re-bought.

**Cancellations are threatened or described in 6 more**: `9059567695` (PT, *"i must unsubscribe"*), `9462122321` (US, *"I'll not renew"*), `10257601209` (US, *"will cancel until this is fixed"*), `13298363427` (IN, cancelled on the last day of the 7-day trial after a developer reply), `14423487007` (US, *"I just cancelled the subscription. That's $29 down the drain"*), `9977790318` (US, *"Wasted $30"*). **Interpretation: subscription billing generates more conflict here than report 43's one-time model — 13 billing failures versus none — but the absolute numbers are still small. The two "charged on day one of the trial" reviews are worth investigating precisely because they describe what reviewers elsewhere call a trial trap.**

## 5.6 Barriers to upgrading — people who wanted to pay and didn't

The barrier is rarely "no money". It is, in descending frequency:

1. **The subscription shape** — `mf_want_one_time_purchase` (53) + `mf_anti_subscription` (41). *"I'm not subscribing to ANOTHER subscription"* (`8962574447`, US, 1★). Now largely answered by the lifetime tier, whose **price** is the new barrier (27, summary finding 3).
2. **A free tier too small to judge** — `mf_trial_too_limited` (32, mean 1.88), §3.4.1.
3. **A named cheaper alternative** — `mf_competitor_cheaper` (28): *"Habit Tracker (7€) oder Streaks (6€) sind aber deutlich günstiger und ähnlich gut… 9,99€ wären doch ok als Einmalkauf"* / "Habit Tracker (€7) or Streaks (€6) are much cheaper and similarly good… €9.99 one-time would be fine" (`9863653291`, DE, 1★); *"Awesome Habits is $22.99 for a LIFETIME license. Habit Tracker (by Davetech Co) is $4.99"* (`8226941128`, US, 4★); *"习惯口袋。功能几乎一样…直接买断只要45"* / "Habit Pocket — almost the same features… buyout is only ¥45" (`8679013164`, CN, 1★).
4. **Regional price level** — `mf_regional_pricing` (8, all non-US): Turkey (`6905795652`, *"premium 179 ₺ çok pahallı"*; `10491720133`, *"your prices for 'Turkey' are quite high"*; `9410319036`), Saudi Arabia (`6775992927`, *"١٠٩ ريال سنويا !!"* / "109 riyals a year!!"), Vietnam (`7470203097`, suggesting ~30,000₫/month), Latin America (`7936681479`, *"la suscripción es demasiado cara en latinoamerica"*), Israel (`9171626582`, cannot buy online at all), Kazakhstan (`13689294667`).
5. **Students and minors** — `seg_student` (4) and `mf_discount_request` (6): *"学生党真的伤不起"* / "students really can't afford it" (`5483060991`, CN); *"I know i'm just a kid and i bought this app but… the only money i get is chore money and i have to wash the windows for it"* (`9723519853`, US, 5★); *"I go to Cambridge on a partially paid scholarship and work part time"* (`10909796403`, GB, 2★); *"please offer discount for students"* (`14014439909`, US, 5★, Apr 2026); *"Có mã giảm giá cho lifetime không"* / "Is there a discount code for lifetime?" (`14104386064`, VN, 5★, May 2026).
6. **Fear the app will disappear** — `mf_longevity_doubt` (5, all CN, summary finding 10).
7. **No Family Sharing** — `10144327227` (DE, 1★, *"es gibt aber keine Familienfreigabe des Abos. Dafür sind 29 eur im Jahr zu teuer"* / "there's no Family Sharing for the subscription; for that, €29 a year is too expensive"), `11059131741` (US, 3★, *"It would be too expensive to have 5 subscriptions so my entire family could use the tool"*).

## 5.7 Competitive position as reviewers describe it

198 reviews describe choosing this app over alternatives (§3.3.5). **The stated basis for choosing it is design plus simplicity; the stated basis for leaving is price.** When reviewers leave, they name: a spreadsheet, a notebook or Apple Reminders (`6154641193`, `6258595511`, `8804092407`, `10931667412`); a cheaper lifetime tracker (`8226941128`, `8679013164`, `9863653291`, `11040174280`, `11488397934`); or a bundle (`6998451648`, *"Done app gave me SIX different apps full feature for only $25 for the year"*; `9882563220`, *"(Not Boring) Habits… costs only for $15 per year with the package of the company's other 3 apps"*). **No reviewer in 2,178 leaves because a rival's design or feature set is better.** One reviewer returned after trying a free alternative: *"After a year of using this habit tracker I tried switching to a free app and within two weeks switched back to Everyday. Yes, it costs a bit of money. However, I realized that a tool that actually motivates me to stick with my habits is worth it"* (`7398777101`, US, 5★). **Interpretation: the product has no design competitor in this corpus — only price competitors.**

**The US mean (4.34) and the non-US mean (4.33) are identical.** Germany (3.95) is the only eligible storefront meaningfully below the corpus mean; India (4.52) is the highest. Signal labels in each section below use **that storefront's own count** as the denominator.

## 6.2 United States — n = 728 (33.43%), mean 4.34

Thresholds at this denominator: 1% = 7.3 reviews, 3% = 21.8, 5% = 36.4.

| Theme | n | % of 728 | Signal (US) | vs global |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cp_simplicity` | 177 | 24.31% | **high-priority** | above global 19.51% |
| `cp_visual_colour_system` | 110 | 15.11% | **high-priority** | **well above** global 10.47% |
| `cp_competitive_displacement` | 93 | 12.77% | **high-priority** | above global 9.09% |
| `mf_free_cap_3habits` | 53 | 7.28% | **high-priority** | below global 8.26% |
| `mf_price_too_high` | 50 | 6.87% | **high-priority** | below global 7.48% |
| `mp_paid_subscription` | 33 | 4.53% | very strong | above global 3.40% |
| `ux_review_prompt_nag` | 31 | 4.26% | very strong | **1.9× global 2.30%** |
| `seg_adhd_neuro_health` | 23 | 3.16% | very strong | **2.3× global 1.38%** |
| `mf_misleading_free_claim` | 12 | 1.65% | meaningful | above global 1.24% |
| `mf_want_one_time_purchase` | 9 | 1.24% | meaningful | **half** global 2.43% |
| `rel_data_loss` | 4 | 0.55% | emerging | **half** global 1.10% |

**Four things are distinctively American in this corpus:**
1. **The rating prompt is felt hardest here.** 31 of 50 prompt complaints (62%) are US against a 33.4% baseline.
2. **The ADHD / neurodivergent segment is American.** 23 of 30 (76.7%). See §6.15.
3. **Americans ask for a one-time purchase far less.** 9 of 53 requests (17.0%) — the subscription shape is the non-US objection (§6.14).
4. **Data loss is under-represented here.** 4 of 24 reports (16.7%). The recent data-loss cluster is Russian (§6.8), not American.

The US also has the highest payer rate of any eligible storefront (44 of 728, **6.04%**) and the most detailed comparison-shopping in the corpus — a reviewer who priced the Awesome Habits and Habit Tracker lifetime licences against this app's subscription (`8226941128`).

## 6.3 China — n = 136 (6.24%), mean 4.23

Thresholds: 1% = 1.4 reviews, 3% = 4.1, 5% = 6.8.

| Theme | n | % of 136 | Signal (CN) | vs global |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `mf_price_too_high` | 17 | 12.50% | **high-priority** | 1.7× global |
| `rel_offline_mode_bug` | 16 | 11.76% | **high-priority** | **15× global 0.78%** |
| `cp_beautiful_design` | 16 | 11.76% | **high-priority** | similar |
| `mf_want_one_time_purchase` | 15 | 11.03% | **high-priority** | **4.5× global 2.43%** |
| `cp_simplicity` | 14 | 10.29% | **high-priority** | **half** global 19.51% |
| `meta_rating_text_mismatch` | 7 | 5.15% | **high-priority** | 3.6× global |
| `rel_crash_freeze` | 6 | 4.41% | very strong | 3.8× global |
| `mf_longevity_doubt` | 5 | 3.68% | very strong | **all 5 in the corpus** |
| `mf_free_cap_3habits` | 4 | 2.94% | meaningful | **a third of** global 8.26% |

**China is the only storefront with its own defect, its own purchase preference, and its own 4★ habit.** The offline-mode cluster, the one-time purchase preference and the abandonment fear are analysed in summary finding 10. Three further points:
- **Chinese reviewers object to the payment *shape*, not the cap.** The cap appears in only 4 CN reviews (2.94%); the one-time ask in 15. *"感觉价格好贵，有没一次性买断，不喜欢订阅暗扣"* / "Feels expensive — is there a one-time buyout? I don't like subscription auto-deductions" (`4888630106`, 3★); *"其他时间计划管理类的app永久买断制的大多¥12，了不起就是¥18，你这只是包年就一百多"* / "Other planning apps with a permanent buyout are mostly ¥12, ¥18 at most — yours is over ¥100 for a single year" (`7151302123`, 5★); *"希望通过降低价格，一次性买断，吸引更多用户，来提高收益"* / "Lower the price and offer a one-time buyout to attract more users and raise revenue" (`5753851503`, 4★).
- **4★ is China's modal complaint rating.** 37 of 136 CN reviews (27.2%) are 4★ against 11.6% globally; Chinese reviewers rate generously while stating a defect.
- **Half of all CN reviews are from 2018–2020** (68 of 136). The storefront's current voice is thinner than its historical one: 27 reviews in 2024–2026.

## 6.4 Spain — n = 127 (5.83%), mean 4.33

Thresholds: 1% = 1.3, 3% = 3.8, 5% = 6.4.

| Theme | n | % of 127 | Signal (ES) | vs global |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cp_behaviour_change` | 24 | 18.90% | **high-priority** | 1.5× global |
| `cp_simplicity` | 23 | 18.11% | **high-priority** | similar |
| `cp_ease_of_use` | 17 | 13.39% | **high-priority** | 1.6× global |
| `mf_free_cap_3habits` | 15 | 11.81% | **high-priority** | 1.4× global; **mean 2.00** |
| `mf_price_too_high` | 9 | 7.09% | **high-priority** | similar |
| `mf_anti_subscription` | 5 | 3.94% | very strong | 2.1× global |

**Spain is the developer's home market and it reads like one.** At least **11 reviews are written entirely in Catalan** (`3221329083`, `4252095651`, `5833679942`, `6528345270`, `6935333300`, `9956172746`, `10309159252`, `10404136654`, `10485601208`, `12834595865`, `13432563724`), two of which praise the Catalan localization by name (*"A més està totalment en català"* / "And it's fully in Catalan" — `9956172746`; *"En català!"* — `13432563724`). Reviewers address the developer directly (*"Enhorabuena Joan!!"* — `6204835609`; *"Felicitats Joan per aquesta app"* — `7935648962`). Against that warmth, **the cap objection is harsher here than anywhere else** — mean 2.00 across 15 reviews: *"Solo te deja 'crear' 3 hábitos sin pagar. Instalada, probada y desinstalada en menos de 2 minutos"* / "It only lets you create 3 habits without paying. Installed, tried and uninstalled in under 2 minutes" (`6389890469`, 1★). And the content gap lands hardest here: the Spanish reviewer who would *"consider subscribing"* if the Learn content were in Spanish (`8985834449`) is the clearest localization-to-revenue statement in the corpus.

## 6.5 Canada — n = 99 (4.55%), mean 4.32

| Theme | n | % of 99 | Signal (CA) | vs global |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cp_simplicity` | 20 | 20.20% | **high-priority** | similar |
| `mf_free_cap_3habits` | 10 | 10.10% | **high-priority** | 1.2× global |
| `cp_learn_content` | 9 | 9.09% | **high-priority** | **1.9× global 4.91%** |
| `mf_price_too_high` | 7 | 7.07% | **high-priority** | similar |
| `cp_cross_platform` | 6 | 6.06% | **high-priority** | 1.8× global |

| `mf_lifetime_price_too_high` | 3 | 3.03% | very strong | 2.4× global |

Canada's price objections are denominated in Canadian dollars and are sharper for it: *"It's like $40/year just to track more than 3 habits. Are you out of your mind???"* (`6914413184`, 4★); *"$9.99 a month · $39.99 per year · $129.99 life time… the prices are not anywhere close to worth it"* (`10772502203`, 1★); *"The price for 129.99$ CAD is way too high for a lifetime purchase"* (`10356458728`, 1★). All three Canadian data-loss reviews are 1★ (`6519048624`, *"both my habits are missing and all of my progress"*; `6998451648`, a $40 annual payer; `10988898467`, account not recognised). **Limited to 99 records; the data-loss finding rests on 3.**

## 6.6 Germany — n = 84 (3.86%), mean 3.95 · **the lowest-rated eligible storefront**

| Theme | n | % of 84 | Signal (DE) | vs global |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cp_simplicity` | 18 | 21.43% | **high-priority** | similar; **mean 5.00** |
| `mf_price_too_high` | 15 | 17.86% | **high-priority** | **2.4× global** |
| `mf_free_cap_3habits` | 14 | 16.67% | **high-priority** | **2.0× global** |
| `cp_beautiful_design` | 12 | 14.29% | **high-priority** | above global; **mean 3.92** |
| `mf_want_one_time_purchase` | 6 | 7.14% | **high-priority** | 2.9× global |
| `mf_anti_subscription` | 6 | 7.14% | **high-priority** | 3.8× global |
| `fr_more_colours` | 6 | 7.14% | **high-priority** | 2.6× global |
| `ux_localization_gap` | 3 | 3.57% | very strong | 6× global |

**Germany is where the subscription model is argued against most intensely.** Every monetization theme runs at two to four times its global rate. *"bis man 'gezwungen' wird… völlig überteuerten Abo-Preis von 4.99 Euro abzuschließen… wo soll man in der heutigen Zeit denn noch alles ein Abo abschließen??? … Dann lieber ein Notizblock für 5 Euro kaufen"* / "until you're 'forced' into a completely overpriced €4.99 subscription… how many subscriptions are we supposed to have these days??? … Better to buy a €5 notepad" (`7922529530`, 2★); *"Gäbe es statt eines Abos die Möglichkeit den vollen Umfang mit einer einmaligen Zahlung zu bekommen, wäre es (je nach Kosten) eine Überlegung auf jeden Fall wert"* / "If there were a one-time payment for the full version instead of a subscription, it would definitely be worth considering, depending on the price" (`6140095778`, 3★); *"es gibt aber keine Familienfreigabe des Abos"* / "there's no Family Sharing for the subscription" (`10144327227`, 1★). The same reviewers love the design — simplicity carries a perfect 5.00 mean here — and give the most precise articulation of the colour mechanic in any language: *"keine Häkchen zum abhaken sondern Flächen die sich in schönen Farben füllen. Dh ich fühle mich nicht gestresst dadurch etwas nicht zu schaffen"* / "not ticks to check off but areas that fill with beautiful colours — so I don't feel stressed about not managing something" (`8314270653`, 5★). Two German reviewers say they cannot use the app because they do not read English (`5973806674`, 2020; `10678104377`, Dec 2023), although German is listed today. **Interpretation: in Germany the product is loved and the business model is disliked, in equal measure. It is the market where a one-time or lifetime price test would be most informative.**

**Russia is the mirror image of Germany on price and the epicentre of the recent data-loss cluster.** Six Russian reviews call the price fair — *"по адекватной цене"* / "at a reasonable price" (`7823007976`), *"за скромные деньги"* / "for modest money" (`12155849294`), *"абсолютно адекватная цена"* / "an absolutely reasonable price" (`12814788584`), *"подписка стоит своих денег"* / "the subscription is worth the money" (`13112620591`) — against zero Russian reviews in the lifetime-price theme. **Interpretation, labelled: consistent with regional price tiers landing well in Russia.** Against that, **6 of the corpus's 24 data-loss reviews are Russian, and all four 2026 data-loss reviews are Russian** (summary finding 6), together with two of the storefront's three sync complaints (`13741293681`, `13765557194`). Earlier Russian instances: `10429731916` (1★, Oct 2023, *"Widgets doesn't not work now. Progress in not saved"*), `10995769246` (2★, Mar 2024, a new habit erased within minutes). **Limited evidence by count (n=6), but the concentration — one storefront, one month, the same symptom from four independent reviewers — is itself the finding.** Russian reviewers also ask most for colours: *"очень жду новые цвета или вообще возможность самостоятельной настройки цвета по коду"* / "I'm really waiting for new colours, or the ability to set colours by code" (`11699632553`, 5★).

## 6.12 High-spend market group

**Definition (external, labelled):** the App Store's largest consumer-spend markets per publicly reported rankings accessed 11 September 2026 — **United States, Japan, China, United Kingdom, Germany, South Korea, Canada, Australia, France** — the same definition as report 43. This is a spend definition, not a download or review-volume definition.

| Metric | High-spend group | Rest of world |
|---|---|---|
| Storefronts present | 9 of 9 | 84 |
| Reviews | **1,265 (58.08%)** | 913 (41.92%) |
| Mean rating | 4.32 | 4.34 |
| **Payers** | **71 (5.61%)** | **26 (2.85%)** |
| `mf_free_cap_3habits` | 100 (7.91%) | 80 (8.76%) |
| `mf_price_too_high` | 107 (8.46%) | 56 (6.13%) |
| `mf_want_one_time_purchase` | 36 (2.85%) | 17 (1.86%) |
| `mf_regional_pricing` | 0 (0.00%) | 8 (0.88%) |
| `ux_review_prompt_nag` | 40 (3.16%) | 10 (1.10%) |
| `cp_simplicity` | 270 (21.34%) | 155 (16.98%) |
| `cp_visual_colour_system` | 153 (12.09%) | 75 (8.21%) |

**Findings.** (a) **The payer rate is twice as high inside the high-spend group** (5.61% vs 2.85%) — the only large behavioural difference between the groups. (b) **The cap objection is the same inside and outside** (7.91% vs 8.76%): it is not a price-sensitivity artefact. (c) **Every regional-price complaint is outside the group.** (d) High-spend reviewers are *more* likely to call the price too high (8.46% vs 6.13%) — driven by Germany and China — while also paying more. **Caveat: the group is 57.5% US by volume (728 of 1,265).** Japan (17), South Korea (22) and France (48) contribute 87 reviews between them and support no standalone conclusion.

## 6.14 English-primary versus other storefronts

Members: US, GB, CA, AU, IN, PH, SG, NZ, IE, ZA, HK, MY, NG — **1,046 reviews (48.03%), mean 4.37**, against 1,132 (51.97%, mean 4.30) from all other storefronts.

| Theme | English-primary | Other storefronts | Direction |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cp_simplicity` | 23.71% | 15.64% | English praises simplicity 1.5× more |
| `cp_visual_colour_system` | 13.58% | 7.60% | English praises the colour grid 1.8× more |

| `rel_offline_mode_bug` | 0.00% | 1.50% | Others only (China) |
| Payers | 5.64% | 3.36% | English pays 1.7× more |

**Interpretation: English-language reviewers articulate the design and pay for it; non-English reviewers like it as much by rating but ask twice as often to own it outright.** That split is the market case for offering the lifetime tier at a lower regional price rather than lowering the subscription.

## 6.15 The ADHD / neurodivergent / chronic-illness segment — 30 reviews (1.38%, meaningful), mean 4.83

This is a segment, not a geography, but it is **76.7% US (23 of 30)** and belongs here. Members name ADHD (18), OCD (2), neurodivergence / "neurospicy" (2), dyslexia (1), bipolar II (1), BPD (1) and chronic illness or pain (2); five more describe executive-function, therapy (CBT) or medication-management use without naming a condition. Two reviews name two conditions. All 30: `4997111034`, `6703493891`, `6877373050`, `9486286103`, `9678067897`, `9942356920`, `9943686378`, `9952382181`, `9957115619`, `9961143974`, `9997347192`, `9997985968`, `10483508663`, `10506700189`, `10730081960`, `10778721049`, `10878622009`, `11153160723`, `11433849366`, `11694795267`, `11839292996`, `11915235002`, `11918274646`, `12129775211`, `12136286563`, `12222718804`, `12515196712`, `12701597104`, `13943168290`, `14091755398`.

**Growth:** 2 of 372 reviews in 2018–2020 (0.54%) → 3 of 401 (0.75%) → 10 of 525 (1.90%) → 15 of 880 (1.70%). **Three mechanisms are named repeatedly by this segment:**
- **The forgiving skip** — `10483508663`, `12222718804`, `13943168290` (*"I have chronic illnesses where I can't always do everything. It's really encouraging to know that one off day doesn't kill your streak"*).
- **The widget** — `9961143974`, `9957115619` (*"Love that it also has good Apple Watch app and iPhone widget integration"*), `9997347192`.
- **Minimal input** — *"It makes tracking habits a simple yes/no input, which is perfect for me and my ADHD because I've quickly become burnt out by decision fatigue on other apps"* (`9957115619`, US, 5★); *"Literally 3 click options: Did you do it? Tap. Did you do some of it? Tap. Tap."* (`12136286563`, US, 5★, who also asks for font controls for *"unique reading needs"*).

Only 3 of the 30 are confirmed payers. **Interpretation: the segment is small but has the highest mean of any segment in the corpus, is growing, and rests on the exact features (skip, widget, one-tap) that Part 8 asks to protect.**

---

# PART 7 — TIME-TREND ANALYSIS

## 7.1 Method

Three documented cuts, all over the same 2,178 records:

1. **By calendar year** (§1.4) — highest resolution; 2018 holds only 12 records.
2. **By four eras, chosen around the one known structural event (the rating-prompt burst):** **E1** 9 Aug 2018 → 31 Dec 2020 (n=372, mean 4.05) · **E2** 1 Jan 2021 → 30 Apr 2023 (n=401, mean 4.01) · **E3** 1 May → 31 Dec 2023 (n=525, mean 4.47 — contains the burst) · **E4** 1 Jan 2024 → 4 Sep 2026 (n=880, mean 4.51). This is the primary trend instrument.
3. **By halves split at the median record** — H1 9 Aug 2018 → 9 Jul 2023 (n=1,089), H2 9 Jul 2023 → 4 Sep 2026 (n=1,089). Equal-n control.

A trend is only labelled if it holds in at least two cuts. Percentages are within-period rates. **Because E3 and E4 are diluted by prompted short positive reviews (Warning 3), every negative-theme rate after April 2023 is biased downward. Where that matters, annual *counts* are shown alongside rates.**

## 7.2 Trend 1 — Paywall friction: the share halved, the count did not · **persistent in substance, high confidence**

| Cut | E1 | E2 | E3 | E4 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Paywall union (cap ∪ trial too limited ∪ misleading ∪ more-free ask) | 13.44% | **16.96%** | 5.14% | 6.70% |
| `mf_free_cap_3habits` | 12.90% | **16.21%** | 4.00% | 5.23% |
| Halves (paywall union) | H1 12.30% | — | — | H2 6.43% |

| Year | 2019 | 2020 | 2021 | 2022 | 2023 | 2024 | 2025 | 2026 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Cap reviews (count) | 10 | 38 | 28 | 26 | 32 | 22 | 18 | 6 |
| Cap rate | 11.6% | 13.9% | 15.8% | **18.6%** | 5.3% | 5.0% | 6.2% | 4.1% |

**Both cuts show a halving; the counts show why.** The cap peaked as a *share* in 2022 and fell sharply in 2023 — the year total review volume quadrupled (140 → 609). Its *count* was 32 in 2023 and 22 in 2024, about where it was in 2021–2022. The underlying product fact — three free habits — is unchanged from the first complaint to the listing today. **Interpretation: nothing was fixed. The complaint is being out-shouted by prompted praise.**

## 7.3 Trend 2 — The one-time ask was answered; its price became the objection · **replaced, high confidence**

| Theme | E1 | E2 | E3 | E4 | H1 → H2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `mf_want_one_time_purchase` | **6.99%** | 4.74% | 0.38% | 0.68% | 4.22% → **0.64%** |
| `mf_lifetime_price_too_high` | 0.00% | 0.75% | 1.52% | **1.82%** | 0.46% → **2.02%** |
| `mp_paid_lifetime` | 0.00% | 0.50% | 0.76% | 1.14% | 0.46% → 1.01% |

The first lifetime purchase is May 2021 (`7299054449`); the first lifetime-price objection is July 2022 (`8838040946`, CN, *"唯一让我犹豫的就是买断的价格偏贵了"* / "the only thing making me hesitate is that the buyout price is on the high side"). By 2024–2026 lifetime-price objections outnumber one-time requests by 16 to 6. **Every cut agrees.** Summary finding 3 gives the anchor prices reviewers name.

## 7.4 Trend 3 — Price objections tracked the 2023 price structure · **persistent, medium confidence**

`mf_price_too_high` by count: 9 (2019), 36 (2020), 22 (2021), 17 (2022), **41 (2023)**, 25 (2024), 9 (2025), 4 (2026). The 2020 peak follows the January 2020 increase from $12/year to $30/year documented in `5471831095`; the 2023 peak coincides with the first appearance of the $7.49 / $29.99 / $99.99 structure in reviews (`9882563220`, May 2023) and with the prompt burst's volume. `mf_price_increase` (5 reviews) records the ladder in reviewers' own words: `5471831095` (2020), `7751115272` (2021, *"too expensive compared to it's past rate"*), `10453985829` (2023, *"it went from $12/year to $3/month to $5/month and now it's almost $8/month and $30/year"*), `10540999641` (2023, *"Leider inzwischen viel zu teuer"* / "unfortunately far too expensive now"), `12403355389` (2025, *"the monthly (not annual) price has gone up to $7 and some change"*). **Medium confidence: the 2023 count is inflated by total volume; the 2025–2026 fall is real in both count and rate.**

## 7.5 Trend 4 — Outages resolved; data integrity got worse · **worsening in severity, medium-high confidence**

| Theme | E1 | E2 | E3 | E4 | H1 → H2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Reliability family | **12.63%** | 10.22% | 4.38% | 4.55% | 8.91% → 4.96% |
| `rel_offline_mode_bug` | **3.76%** | 0.25% | 0.38% | 0.00% | 1.47% → 0.09% |
| `rel_crash_freeze` | **3.76%** | 1.50% | 0.38% | 0.34% | 1.93% → 0.37% |
| `rel_data_loss` | 0.81% | 1.00% | 0.76% | **1.48%** | 0.73% → **1.47%** |
| Data-integrity union | 1.08% | **3.99%** | 1.90% | 2.95% | 2.11% → **3.03%** |

**Two opposite movements inside one family.** The outage defects of 2019–2020 (the Chinese offline cluster, the September 2020 won't-open cluster) are gone. The integrity defects — data loss, sync disagreement, stale widgets, accidental taps — rose from 1.08% to 2.95% and doubled across halves **despite the prompt dilution**, which means their true rise is larger than shown. Data loss by count: 0, 3, 2, 1, 5, 5, 4, 4 (2019–2026); **2026's four reports in 146 reviews (2.7%) is the highest annual rate in the corpus.** **Interpretation: the product became more stable to open and less trustworthy to rely on — the worse direction for an app whose value is an unbroken record.**

## 7.6 Trend 5 — The rating prompt · **active, high confidence**

| Theme | E1 | E2 | E3 | E4 | H1 → H2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ux_review_prompt_nag` | 0.00% | 0.25% | **5.71%** | 2.16% | 2.20% → 2.39% |
| `meta_low_information` | 3.76% | 6.23% | **16.00%** | 9.32% | 9.09% → 9.73% |
| `meta_solicited` | 0.00% | 0.00% | 1.52% | 1.02% | 0.46% → 1.10% |

The prompt complaint appears once in January 2022 (`8258958104`) and then arrives with the May 2023 burst. It has **not decayed to zero**: 8 complaints in 2024, 7 in 2025, 4 in the first eight months of 2026. Contentless reviews rise and stay elevated. **Interpretation: the prompt's frequency was reduced after mid-2023 but not bounded. It continues to produce a small, steady stream of complaints and a large stream of low-information reviews.**

## 7.7 Trend 6 — Shipped requests closed and turned into praise · **improving, high confidence**

| Capability | Request theme | Praise theme | Evidence of shipping |
|---|---|---|---|
| Home-screen widgets | `fr_widget`: 4 → 4 → **0 → 0** | `cp_widget`: 0.81% → 3.24% → 2.86% → **3.98%** | `6455669260` (Sept 2020, iOS 14 widgets) |
| Apple Watch | `fr_apple_watch`: 3 → 2 → **0 → 0** | named in `cp_cross_platform` | `7142563934` (Mar 2021) |
| Journal / notes / calendar | `fr_notes_journal` mostly pre-2024 | `cp_journal_notes`: 0 → 0 → 0.19% → **1.25%** | `10762490298`, `10940916205` (Dec 2023 – Feb 2024) |
| Folders / groups | `fr_grouping_tags` 2020–2025 | folder praise 2025 | `12987527653` (Aug 2025), `13116954771` |
| No-account use | `mf_account_required`: 8 complaints, **none after Oct 2022** | no-account praise | `11481910041` (2024), `13247499575` (2025) |
| Chinese / Turkish UI | `ux_localization_gap` UI requests end 2021 | — | `9410319036` (Dec 2022); listing |
| China connectivity | `rel_offline_mode_bug` | — | two instances after 2020 |

**Interpretation: this developer works the request queue, and the corpus records the payoff each time a request closes. The two largest open requests — colours and scheduling — are the next candidates.**

## 7.8 Trend 7 — The early-adopter voice faded · **medium confidence**

`cp_learn_content` **11.02% → 5.24% → 3.05% → 3.30%** (halves 6.61% → 3.21%); `seg_web_or_extension_user` **2.15% → 2.24% → 0.76% → 0.23%**; `sup_responsive_praise` **4.30% → 3.49% → 1.33% → 1.82%**; `mp_support_indie` 12 of 13 in H1; `mp_free_tier_sufficient` **5.11% → 2.84%**. Every cut agrees on direction. **Medium confidence**, because the prompt dilution alone would lower all of these rates; what distinguishes them is that their *counts* also fall (web users: 8, 9, 4, 2). **Interpretation: the product's early community — web-first, content-reading, developer-contacting — has been replaced by a mobile, widget-first audience. The Learn library that community valued is still there and still praised when found.**

## 7.9 Trend 8 — Neurodivergent use grew · **emerging, medium confidence**

`seg_adhd_neuro_health` **0.54% → 0.75% → 1.90% → 1.70%**; halves 1.10% → 1.65%. Both cuts agree, and the counts rise (2, 3, 10, 15). The first ADHD mention is October 2019 (`4997111034`). **Medium confidence: n=30.**

## 7.10 Trend 9 — Friction with the developer's public voice appeared late · **worsening, low-medium confidence**

`sup_dev_reply_hostile`: 0, 1, 0, **5** across eras. `sup_review_pressure`: 2 (May 2023, Sep 2024). `sup_unresponsive`: 0, 3, 0, **4**. **Low-medium confidence: n=15 across the three themes.** The direction is consistent and all but two of the fifteen are 1★ or 2★.

## 7.11 What did not change

- **The free tier: 3 habits**, from `3621518582` (Jan 2019) to the listing on 11 September 2026.
- **Simplicity is the top theme in every era and still rising** — 16.40% → 17.71% → 19.62% → 21.59%.
- **The colour grid is praised at a near-constant rate** — 9.68% → 10.22% → 11.62% → 10.23%.
- **Behaviour change is reported at a near-constant rate** — 11.56% → 13.97% → 14.10% → 12.50%, mean 4.93.
- **More colours has been requested in every era since 2020** — 1.61% → 3.74% → 3.43% → 2.27%.
- **Flexible scheduling has been requested in every era** — 1.08% → 2.00% → 1.71% → 1.48%; first `4841212766` (BR, Sep 2019), latest `14316211160` (CO, Jul 2026).
- **The skip-symbol confusion is seven years old** — `4850070969` (Sep 2019) → `13762762742` (Feb 2026).

---

# PART 8 — PRODUCT IMPLICATIONS

Every recommendation below cites the findings that support it. The ordering is by evidence weight × severity × cost, not by theme size.

## 8.1 Immediate fixes — evidence is unambiguous

| # | Action | Evidence | Why now |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Bound the in-app rating prompt: never in the first week or the first N check-ins, never twice per app version, never again after the user has rated or dismissed it once, never for subscribers, and never on a second device after the first.** | `ux_review_prompt_nag` 50 (2.30%, mean 3.22) including 10 one-stars and 11 three-stars (§3.4.4, §4.3); `meta_solicited` 17; still live on 13 Aug 2026 (`14423865619`); the June 2023 "fixed" report (`10008701823`) was followed by 19 more complaints | The cheapest fix in this report. The prompt is lowering the rating it exists to raise, and it filled the 2023–2026 corpus with contentless reviews that make every later signal harder to read |
| 2 | **Treat the February–March 2026 Russian data-loss cluster as an incident; ship a per-habit change history with one-tap restore ("undo last 7 days"), and make cross-device conflict resolution never delete a completed mark.** | `rel_data_loss` 24 (1.10%, mean 2.12), rising 0.73% → 1.47% across halves; 8 of 24 are payers; four RU reports in 23 days (`13721978399`, `13741293681`, `13765557194`, `13802922048`); data-integrity union 56 (2.57%) | Highest-severity finding. The product's value is an unbroken visual record; silent rewrites destroy it. The Russian cluster is recent enough to still be live |
| 3 | **Audit trial and entitlement flows end-to-end: no charge on trial start; one entitlement across iPhone, iPad, Watch, Mac and web; a visible "restore purchase" and "subscription status" screen.** | `mf_billing_problem` 13 (0.60%, mean 2.31): charged on day one of a trial (`11022906761`, `12285802750`), subscription not recognised (`6863161594`, `6866992048`, `14423487007`), activation failure (`11462388063`), wrong amount (`10373652027`) | Each of these is a paying customer lost at the moment of payment. Two describe what other corpora call a trial trap |
| 4 | **Adopt a reply policy for price reviews: thank, state the free tier and lifetime option factually, never argue value, never ask a reviewer to change their rating.** | `sup_dev_reply_hostile` 6 (mean 1.33), `sup_review_pressure` 2; `13298363427` downgraded from 2★ to 1★ *because of* the reply; `11709301622` *"the developer keeps contacting me to pressure me"* | Zero cost. Every one of these reviews became worse after the developer engaged |
| 5 | **Remove "free forever" from the listing and website, and add one screenshot of the free tier's actual 3-habit view.** | `mf_misleading_free_claim` 27 (1.24%, **mean 1.85 — the lowest of any theme with n>20**): `5534964979`, `6856428999`, `8680118578`, `10931667412`, `11692689432`, `12132723234` | A copy change. It targets the angriest cluster in the corpus |
| 6 | **Add a one-screen legend for the half-square, triangle and faded marks, reachable from the grid.** | `ux_symbols_confusing` 19 (0.87%); the same question from Sep 2019 (`4850070969`) to Feb 2026 (`13762762742`); a subscriber threatening to cancel over it (`10257601209`) | Seven years of the same question; the answer already exists in the product |
| 7 | **Return dark mode to the free tier.** | `mf_paywalled_capability` 8 (mean 2.12); `11382464605` (*"New update moved 'dark mode' behind a paywall. Immediate deletion"*), `11054384115`, `6139789378` | Small revenue, high irritation, and it lands in reviews the week it changes |
| 8 | **After a trial ends, let the user choose which 3 habits stay active and archive — not lock — the rest.** | `mf_locked_out_after_trial` 4: `5695267165`, `6154641193`, `8681575995` (*"Jedoch kann ich sie nicht löschen… So wird die Statistik nämlich verfälscht"* / "I can't delete them… it falsifies the statistics"), `9243772066` (*"I now have a bunch of habits on my screen now that I can't get rid of"*) | Weak by count, but it is the post-trial first impression for every non-converter |

## 8.2 Structural changes — high confidence in direction

| # | Action | Evidence | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 9 | **Raise the free tier from 3 to 4 habits.** | `mf_free_cap_3habits` 180 (8.26%, high-priority, mean 2.43), 41.0% of one-stars; `mf_more_free_habits_request` 29 — every reviewer who names a number names 4–7; the widget holds 4 rows and the free tier cannot fill it (`9997347192`, `12334331759`); report 43 shows a loosened cap removing the theme within two years | The smallest change that fixes the widget defect and answers the modal counter-offer. It does not touch the habit-science argument that 66 reviewers make *for* a small free tier (§3.4.1) |
| 10 | **Ship a larger palette, a custom-colour (hex) picker, and softer / pastel sets; consider a one-time palette pack as a paid add-on.** | `fr_more_colours` 59 (2.71%, mean 4.36; 50 of 59 are 4–5★); `ux_colour_gets_muddy` 4; two reviewers volunteer to pay (`9952382181`, `12101755389`) | The most-requested feature, it sits on the differentiator (§3.3.3), and it is the only unprompted IAP idea in the corpus |
| 11 | **Add every-N-days, X-times-per-week (any days), and monthly schedules; allow a habit to be completed several times a day; allow numeric targets.** | `fr_flexible_frequency` 34 (1.56%, mean 4.09), `fr_multiple_per_day` 12, `fr_counter_quantity` 9; medication every other day (`10205085824`), shift work (`10429962976`), churn to Apple Reminders (`8804092407`) | Requested in every era (§7.11). Mean 4.09: retention, not acquisition |
| 12 | **Distinguish "missed" from "not recorded", and offer an optional one-step decay instead of a full colour reset on a miss — but test it with the neurodivergent segment first.** | `fr_missed_vs_blank` 6 (mean 4.67): `5916248950`, `10089231057`, `12065748660`, `12126118988`, `13783066497`; the segment that relies on the forgiving skip (§6.15, 30 reviews, mean 4.83) | A change to the core mechanic; it must not make a miss feel more like failure |
| 13 | **Localize the Learn content, the 7-day email course and the videos — Spanish first.** | `ux_localization_gap` 13; the Spanish reviewer who would subscribe if the content were in Spanish (`8985834449`); Russian (`9992608962`), Arabic (`11171579515`), French (`6821741061`), Catalan/Spanish emails (`6821437095`) | The UI is already in 15 languages; the content — the thing early users praised most — is not. Spain is the developer's home market (§6.4) |
| 14 | **Allow offline check-off in the free tier, syncing when back online.** | `rel_offline_use_blocked` 7 (mean 2.14) + offline in `mf_paywalled_capability`: `7098518884`, `9029601554`, `10143971103`, `13460787737` | Requiring a connection to tap a square reads as a paywall on the core loop |
| 15 | **Offer Family Sharing on the annual and lifetime plans.** | `mf_no_family_sharing` 2 (`10144327227`, `11059131741`) | Ignore-level by count, but it is a StoreKit setting, and one reviewer names five family members |

## 8.3 What not to change

| # | Guidance | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 16 | **Do not add complexity to justify the price.** | `cp_simplicity` 425 (19.51%), rising every era; *"A lot of great apps start this way, then they start adding weird features that nobody wants for the sake of it, pls dont do that"* (`12631375398`); `cp_no_gamification` 3 all 5★. Justify price with ownership options (rec. E1) and colour (rec. 10), not features |
| 17 | **Do not remove or harden the forgiving skip.** | `cp_skip_forgiving` 41 (mean 4.93); the neurodivergent segment's named mechanism (§6.15); *"that feels like neutral data instead of a personal failing"* (`10483508663`) |
| 18 | **Keep the interactive widget, but add a lock / view-only mode to stop accidental taps.** | `cp_widget` 66 (mean 4.79) vs `rel_accidental_tap` 3 (`10386394785`, `11108703405`, `13783066497`) |
| 19 | **Do not gate further existing capabilities.** | The dark-mode episodes (rec. 7) each produced immediate 1–2★ reviews; `mf_paywalled_capability` mean 2.12 |

## 8.4 Experiments worth running

| # | Experiment | Question it answers | Success measure |
|---|---|---|---|
| E1 | **Lifetime price test at $49 and $69** against the $99.99 control, in a subset of storefronts | Reviewers independently name $30–70 as the lifetime price at which they would buy (`10650959929`, `11735469517`, `10453985829`, `12101755389`); `mf_lifetime_price_too_high` 27 and rising (§7.3) | Lifetime conversion × price; incidence of lifetime-price complaints |
| E2 | **Regional lifetime pricing** for Turkey, Brazil, Latin America, Vietnam, China, Saudi Arabia | `mf_regional_pricing` 8 (all non-US); non-English storefronts ask for one-time purchase 2.1× more (§6.14); Russia, apparently on a lower tier, calls the price fair (§6.8) | Payer rate by storefront; regional-price complaints |
| E3 | **4-habit vs 3-habit free tier** as a cohort test | Whether rec. 9 costs conversions; the corpus cannot say (§8.5) | Trial start rate, conversion, incidence of paywall-union language |
| E4 | **Paid palette pack** (one-time IAP) | Whether colour demand is monetizable outside the subscription | Attach rate; change in `fr_more_colours` incidence |
| E5 | **Student / youth discount** | `seg_student` 4 + `mf_discount_request` 6 (`5483060991`, `9723519853`, `10909796403`, `14014439909`, `10389679638`) | Conversion among verified students |
| E6 | **Resurface the Learn tab after the first 14 days of use**, not only at onboarding | Learn-content praise fell from 11.02% to 3.30% (§7.8) while its per-mention mean stayed 4.82 | Learn-tab opens after day 14; retention at day 60 |

## 8.5 Research questions this corpus raises but cannot answer

1. **What changed for Russian users in February 2026?** Four independent data-loss reports in 23 days from one storefront (§6.8). **Resolvable from sync-server and release logs.**
2. **Was the 2019–2020 China "offline mode" cluster a network-reachability issue?** 16 of 17 reports are Chinese, clustered in two waves (§3.4.3). **Resolvable from connection telemetry by region.**
3. **What is the actual trial-to-paid conversion rate, and what share of payers choose lifetime?** 97 reviewers say they paid; that is a sample of writers. **No conversion claim is made anywhere in this report.**
4. **Did the May 2023 rating prompt move the store's displayed rating?** The corpus shows the prompt produced 297 reviews at mean 4.58 in 44 days; it cannot see the ratings-only population. **Resolvable from rating history.**
5. **Does free-tier size change conversion?** Report 43's app loosened its cap and the complaint vanished; this app did not. **Resolvable only by experiment E3.**
6. **Why is the payer rate twice as high in high-spend markets** (5.61% vs 2.85%) and highest in the US (6.04%)? Possibly price tiers, possibly reporting behaviour. **Resolvable from per-storefront revenue.**
7. **When exactly was dark mode gated?** Reviewers report it paywalled in 2020 and "moved behind a paywall" again in June 2024. **Resolvable from release notes.**

**Boundary rules.**
- **Purchase evidence (`mp_paid_subscription`, `mp_paid_lifetime`) and the payer segment** require first-person evidence — "I paid", "I subscribed", "I bought the lifetime", "I'm a subscriber", a price paid, or a reported charge. Recommending that others pay, "I will buy it", or describing the price in the third person does **not** count. The payer segment in Part 5 is the union of those two themes with `mf_billing_problem` (every billing report involves a charge). This is deliberately conservative; the true payer count is certainly higher than 97.
- **`mf_free_cap_3habits` vs `mf_trial_too_limited` vs `mf_misleading_free_claim` vs `mf_more_free_habits_request`:** the first is any mention of the 3-habit limit in any tone; the second is the argument that the free tier is too small to evaluate; the third is the accusation that the listing or website misrepresents the app as free; the fourth is a named counter-offer (4, 5, 6, 7 habits). A review may carry all four.
- **`mf_price_too_high` vs `neg_value_scepticism`:** the first is "too expensive"; the second is "it does nothing a spreadsheet, a notebook or Reminders can't" — an argument about value, not amount.
- **`cp_replaces_paper` vs `neg_value_scepticism`:** "this replaced my paper tracker" is praise; "I'll go back to paper / a spreadsheet instead" is value scepticism. Eight records were corrected on this rule during drafting (§9.3).
- **`rel_data_loss` vs `rel_sync_issues` vs `rel_widget_broken`:** data loss is marks or history disappearing or being rewritten; sync is devices disagreeing; widget broken is the widget absent, blank, stale or showing wrong data. A review describing a widget that wipes marks carries both widget and data-loss themes.
- **`cp_generic_positive` vs `meta_low_information`:** generic positive requires some substance (a use case, a comparison, a stated benefit) without a nameable driver; low information is a review with no recoverable content ("Good", "👍", "hao", keyboard-mash). A review carries both only when a substantive clause sits beside a contentless one.
- **`meta_rating_text_mismatch`** is assigned only when the star rating plainly contradicts the text (a 5★ report that the app won't open; a 1★ "Love this app!"). It is not assigned for mild disagreements.
- **`meta_solicited`** requires the reviewer to say they are writing because the app asked; it is not inferred from timing.

## 9.4 Counting rules, denominators and reconciliation summary

- **Global denominator is always 2,178.** Other denominators used: each eligible storefront's own count (§6), **1,450** (non-US), **97** (payer segment), **2,081** (non-payers), **250** (1–2★ reviews), **166** (1★), **372 / 401 / 525 / 880** (eras), **1,089 / 1,089** (halves), per-year counts (§1.4), **297 / 1,881** (burst window / rest), and the market groups (1,265 / 913; 1,511 / 667; 1,046 / 1,132). Every segment rate is labelled as a segment rate and shown alongside its global equivalent.
- **Theme counts are non-exclusive.** Family aggregates (§3.2) and the named unions (paywall, pricing model, data integrity, payers) are computed by set union over review IDs, never by summing theme counts.
- **No deduplication was performed.** The three identical (title, body) groups are different reviewers (§1.3).
- **No review was excluded from any denominator** — including the 297 burst reviews, the 205 contentless reviews and the 31 rating/text contradictions. Where those records would distort a figure, the figure is given with and without them.
- **Verification of every cited ID:** all review IDs appearing anywhere in this report were checked against `reviews.jsonl` — **every cited ID exists; zero unknown IDs.** Every §9.7 row was checked against the classification map and the source record (ID, storefront, rating, date, themes). Every §3.1 count, percentage and mean was recomputed from the map. All passed (`temp/46-verify-report.py`).
- **Reconciliation:** 2,178 records in `reviews.jsonl` = 2,178 summed across 93 `by_country/*.jsonl` files = 2,178 in `manifest.json`; per-country counts, rating distribution and mean all reproduce exactly.

## 9.5 Provenance of this analysis

| Artifact | Path | Contents |
|---|---|---|
| Classification map | `temp/46-review-classification.py` | The hand-curated `review_id → [themes]` map for all 2,178 records, the 109-theme taxonomy with definitions, and every post-read patch with its reason. **Every number in this report derives from this one file.** |
| Working corpus | `temp/46-all-reviews.txt` | All 2,178 reviews, entity-unescaped, in date order — the file that was read |
| Corpus dump | `temp/46-dump.py` | Builds the working corpus and prints the profile stats |
| Reconciliation | `temp/46-reconcile.py` | The §1.3 checks |
| Validation | `temp/46-validate.py` | Classification integrity checks |
| Aggregation | `temp/46-aggregate.py`, `temp/46-trends.py` | Counts, cross-tabs, payers, storefronts, market groups, eras, halves, years, burst window |
| Table generation | `temp/46-mastertable.py`, `temp/46-gen-appendix.py`, `temp/46-build-report.py` | §3.1, §3.2, §4.6, §9.1, §9.3, §9.7, §9.8 and the report assembly |
| Verification | `temp/46-verify-report.py` | Post-write ID, index and master-table checks |
